Using the optical [N II]/H$α$ BPT diagram, we find that composite and LINER-like excitation together account for most of the classified area (median fractions 0.44 and 0.26), while Seyfert-like gas covers a median fraction of 0.02 but carries a far larger proportion of the line luminosity where it is present. Both the projected size of the Seyfert-classified region, $R_{\rm Sy}=0.08$-$7.5$ kpc, and its [O III] luminosity increase with the bolometric AGN luminosity, with $R_{\rm Sy}\propto L_{\rm AGN,bol}^{0.54\pm0.18}$, as expected for gas photoionized at a constant ionization parameter. The scatter about the luminosity relation depends on dust opacity: the [O III] luminosity per unit AGN luminosity falls by $1.09\pm0.25$ dex per unit depth of the 9.7 $μ$m silicate absorption feature (Spearman $ρ=-0.60$, $p=0.001$). **Authors:** Daniel Majaess

**Abstract:** The Leavitt & Pickering (1912) mean period-magnitude relation for SMC Cepheids exhibits correlated residuals relative to modern Gaia $B_G$ and Johnson $V$ photometry ($r_{δV/δL12}\approx0.76$). Therefore dispersion inherent to Leavitt's photographic photometry relation is not solely random scatter ($σ_{L12,B_G,V}=0.22\pm0.03,0.25\pm0.04,0.20\pm0^{m}.03$), and is potentially consistent with the partial encoding of combined effects from the elongated SMC, classical Cepheid instability strip, differential reddening, etc. OGLE data indicate that $\gtrsim870$ SMC variables identified by Leavitt (1908, Table 2) are classical Cepheids ($\gtrsim 90$ %), and the preliminary unphased Table 2 photometry likewise features correlated residuals relative to contemporary observations ($r_{δB_G/δL08-T2}\approx0.54$). Table 6 data in Leavitt (1908, $n=16$) define a highly linear Leavitt law over an extensive baseline ($r\approx-0.98$), but constitute a smaller sample than the later 1912 work ($n=25$).

## 55. [Extreme mass ratio inspirals from the Hills mechanism, The eccentricity distribution of Hills EMRIs in LISA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13294) <a id="link55"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.13294

**Authors:** Biancamaria Sersante, Davide Mancieri, Kevin Leopoldo Muñoz Zavala, Luca Broggi, Zephyr Penoyre, Elena Maria Rossi, Alberto Sesana

**Abstract:** Extreme mass ratio inspirals (EMRIs) are promising sources for future space-based gravitational-wave detectors. We investigate EMRIs produced through the Hills mechanism, in which a stellar-mass black-hole binary (BBH) is tidally separated by a massive black hole (MBH), leaving one component bound to it. We present the first self-consistent modelling of this channel, combining an orbit-averaged Fokker-Planck treatment of the nuclear star cluster (NSC), three-body modelling of the binary-MBH encounter, Monte Carlo evolution of captured black holes (BHs), and fully relativistic EMRI trajectories to follow the evolution from binary supply down to final plunge. We find that Hills EMRIs are substantially more circular than classical EMRIs formed through two-body relaxation. For an MBH of $4 \times 10^6 M_\odot$ and circular, equal-mass BBHs of $20 M_\odot$ with initial separation of $0.1$ AU, EMRI mean eccentricities are approximately $0.097$ at our adopted LISA-band entry threshold and $0.034$ at plunge, compared with $0.478$ and $0.228$, respectively, for classical EMRIs. Of the initial BBHs, approximately $0.4$ per cent become EMRIs, whereas approximately $60$ per cent of captured BHs do. For comparable NSC initial compact-object mass, we find approximately $1.6 \times 10^{-8}$ Hills EMRIs per year per galaxy, approximately $12$ per cent of the classical EMRI yield. The Hills channel contribution to the overall eccentricity distribution depends strongly on the binary mass fraction in NSCs. We discuss implications for identifying EMRI formation channels with LISA and for quasi-periodic eruptions.

## 56. [TDCOSMO 2026: new insights on the Hubble tension from strong lensing time delays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14881) <a id="link56"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.14881

**Authors:** TDCOSMO Collaboration, Simon Birrer, Michele Cappellari, Frédéric Courbin, Christopher D. Fassnacht, Pierre Fleury, Joshua A. Frieman, Aymeric Galan, Xiang-Yu Huang, Daniel P. Johnson, Sanjana Khanolkar, Shawn Knabel, Hrvoje Krizic, Martin Millon, Takahiro Morishita, Veronica Motta, Pritom Mozumdar, Eric Paic, Hadrien Paugnat, Allan G. Schweinfurth, Anowar J. Shajib, William Sheu, Dominique Sluse, Massimo Stiavelli, Sherry H. Suyu, Tommaso Treu, Felipe Urcelay, Devon M. Williams, Kenneth C. Wong

**Abstract:** We discuss the measurement of the Hubble constant ($H_0$) in flat $Λ$ cold dark matter cosmology based on the blind hierarchical analysis of 11 multiply-imaged quasars (hereafter the TDCOSMO-2026 sample) and described in greater detail in a companion paper. In addition to the time delays, we use lens models based on high-resolution imaging from the Hubble Space Telescope (HST) and from the James Webb Space Telescope (JWST), analyzed jointly with axisymmetric dynamical models based on JWST-NIRSpec, VLT-MUSE, and Keck-KCWI spectroscopy. Our primary result $H_0=71.1^{+2.4}_{-2.3}$ km.s$^{-1}$.Mpc$^{-1}$ is based on the TDCOSMO-2026 sample, in combination with $Ω_{\rm m}$ constraints from the DES-Dovekie supernova (SN) dataset. Our 3% precision accounts for the mass-sheet degeneracy. Importantly, our measurement is independent of the local distance ladder (LDL). Among individual late-universe probes, only the traditional LDL is more precise. We discuss our result in the context of the Hubble tension, the difference between $H_0$ measured in the late universe and the prediction based on early-universe probes such as the cosmic microwave background (CMB). We construct combinations of independent late-universe probes and show that the tension with the Planck CMB prediction is robust to excluding any one of the methods based on Type-Ia Supernovae, Cepheids, or the Tip of the Red Giant Branch. For example, we construct a 'no-SN' $H_0$ measurement, by replacing the DES-Dovekie constraint on $Ω_{\rm m}$ with that from DESI baryon acoustic oscillations, and then combining TDCOSMO-2026 with independent non-SN late-universe probes. This 'no-SN' late-universe probe is in 3.7$σ$ tension with Planck. Even after completely excluding the LDL, the tension with Planck persists at the 2.7$σ$ level.

## 57. [CosmoAgent: AI Agent for Theoretical Cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16536) <a id="link57"></a>

**Authors:** Stephon Alexander, Loukas Gouskos, Savvas M. Koushiappas, Cooper Niu, Wenrong Sun

**Abstract:** We present CosmoAgent, an agentic AI framework using large language models (LLMs) to search for dark-energy and modified-gravity models. We define a covariant grammar that allows LLM agents to explore the space of modified-gravity Lagrangians. An in-house computational pipeline derives the equations of motion, solves the background dynamics, and evaluates the likelihood using late-time expansion data, including the DES 5-year type Ia supernova sample and the DESI Data Release 2 BAO measurements. Theories are ranked by the Akaike Information Criterion (AIC) to balance simplicity and likelihood. We benchmarked 8 frontier LLMs from ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini, Kimi, DeepSeek, and Qwen, and all LLMs are able to find theories with a lower nominal AIC than flat $Λ$CDM, with a best improvement of $Δ$ AIC=-3.328. Seven recover quadratic quintessence. Exploratory searches also return induced-gravity and scalar-Gauss-Bonnet structures. The modest AIC improvements do not establish an observational preference for these models over flat $Λ$CDM. The code is publicly available at https://github.com/wsunamoutlook-prog/AI-Modified-Gravity

## 58. [Vanishing Love Numbers of Kerr-Newman Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17179) <a id="link58"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17179

**Authors:** Sumanta Chakraborty, Takuya Katagiri, Paolo Pani, Jun-Xi Shi

**Abstract:** Tidal Love numbers characterize the conservative response of compact objects to external perturbations and provide a powerful probe of their nature. While the static Love numbers of Schwarzschild, Kerr, and Reissner--Nordström black holes remarkably vanish, the tidal response of a generic Kerr--Newman black hole has remained unexplored due to the intrinsic coupling between gravitational and electromagnetic perturbations. Exploiting a recent breakthrough in the separability of these perturbations, we analytically determine the static tidal response of the most general stationary black hole in four-dimensional Einstein--Maxwell theory. For arbitrary multipole order, charge, and spin, we show that the entire matrix of conservative Love numbers vanishes, including the mixed gravito-electromagnetic couplings. We also compute the nonvanishing static dissipative response induced by frame dragging.

## 59. [Relaxation Dark Energy: Compositional Dynamics and the Future of Cosmic Acceleration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13739) <a id="link59"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.13739

**Authors:** Gianmassimo Tasinato

**Abstract:** If cosmic acceleration is driven by a positive cosmological constant, the universe approaches an eternally accelerating de Sitter state, whose interpretation in quantum gravity remains elusive. We develop a framework in which acceleration is instead a transient stage of dark-sector relaxation towards compositional equilibrium: matter and dark energy approach a stationary density ratio, eventually diluting like pressureless matter. Guided by linear response theory and Onsager's near-equilibrium regression hypothesis, we describe this evolution through a composition variable measuring departure from equilibrium. Extending constant fractional relaxation to the full background evolution yields the dark-energy equation of state $w=w_0a^{-n}$, where $n$ is the relaxation rate per e-fold of expansion. This history connects early dark-energy suppression, an upward phantom crossing and a density maximum to a pressureless scaling future. Using DESI DR2 BAO and Planck distance priors, with and without three alternative supernova compilations, we find a nominal $2.6$--$3.6σ$ preference for nonzero relaxation over constant $w$. The composition framework also motivates a constitutive prescription for linear perturbations that remains regular at phantom crossing. Near equilibrium, regular long-wavelength composition disturbances relax at the background rate to leading order, while small-scale fluctuations propagate as damped sound waves. We prove, however, that for separately conserved matter and dark energy, the pointwise constitutive relation cannot sustain a regular, gradient-stable negative-pressure branch throughout its full past evolution. We explore modifications of the pressure response and their consistency with neighbouring homogeneous histories, identifying requirements for extending the near-equilibrium description to the full cosmological evolution.

## 60. [Direct Observational Constraint on Upstream Magnetic Field Amplification Reveals the Particle Acceleration Limit in a Supernova Remnant](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16296) <a id="link60"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16296

**Authors:** Yuya Kawabata, Takaaki Tanaka, Hiromasa Suzuki, Tsuyoshi Inoue

**Abstract:** The origin of Galactic cosmic rays up to the knee energy (PeV) remains a long-standing open question in astrophysics. Supernova remnant shocks are leading candidates for accelerating particles to PeV energies, yet this requires strong magnetic field amplification in the upstream region to confine the highest energy particles. Despite its importance, the upstream magnetic field strength has long lacked direct observational constraints. Here we report the observation of numerous synchrotron X-ray hotspots extending into the upstream of the supernova remnant RX J1713.7$-$3946 in archival Chandra data. These hotspots show spatial and temporal properties consistent with synchrotron emission from secondary electrons produced by hadronic interactions between accelerated protons and dense molecular clumps. Using the spatial extent of these upstream hotspots as a physical scale, we estimate the proton diffusion length to be $\sim 1~\mathrm{pc}$. This measurement provides a constraint on the upstream magnetic field strength of $\sim 10~μ\mathrm{G}$, which is consistent with recent simulations of Bell-instability-driven magnetic field amplification. Our results indicate that the current amplification is insufficient to reach PeV energies, explaining the observed TeV gamma-ray spectral cutoff and providing a diagnostic for assessing PeVatron capability in stellar shocks.

**Authors:** Mingxuan Xu, Jiang-Tao Li, Li Ji, Chen Zhang, Yantian Zhu

**Abstract:** Wide-field narrowband imaging provides an efficient approach for mapping low-surface-brightness line emission over large angular scales. We present initial performance tests of Multi-Application Survey Telescope Array (MASTA) for narrowband imaging. Using commissioning data obtained with the QHY461 and COSMOS-8K cameras, we characterize the basic reduction procedure, photometric calibration, image quality, and point-source detection limits. The measured surface-brightness and point-source detection limits become deeper with increasing exposure time, roughly following the expected background-limited scaling. Under the current observing and reduction conditions, the COSMOS-8K camera generally reaches deeper surface-brightness limits, particularly in the red filters, while QHY461 has smaller pixel size and provides better sampling of the telescope point spread function (PSF). As demonstrations, we perform continuum subtraction for the nearby galaxy M106 and the supernova remnant Cygnus Loop using adjacent narrowband filters and reveal the Hα and [O III] structures in M106 and filamentary structures in the Cygnus Loop. We further compare the MASTA surface brightness Hα map of M106 with an independent SITELLE measurement. After accounting for [N II] contamination and the wavelength-dependent response of the 6544 Å filter in the f /1.7 converging beam, the corrected median ratio is 0.973. These tests indicate that MASTA is a promising facility for wide-field narrowband imaging. Further improvements in calibration frames, including flux calibration, field-dependent bandpass characterization, and spatially varying PSF modeling and matching will be required for quantitative studies of diffuse ionized gas.

## 62. [Assessing PLATO's Discovery Potential across Different Observing Strategies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13686) <a id="link62"></a>

**Authors:** Yoshi Nike Emilia Eschen, Michelle Kunimoto, Juan Cabrera, Adrien Leleu, Thomas G. Wilson, Heike Rauer, Alex Romanec, François Bouchy, Magali Deleuil, Nicholas Jannsen, Don Pollacco, Toby Rodel, Sophia Sulis, Stéphane Udry

**Abstract:** The PLATO mission, planned to launch in spring 2027, will observe a fixed Southern field, LOPS2, for at least 2 years with the goal of discovering and deriving precise parameters for planets as small as Earth around FGK dwarfs and sub-giants. Afterwards, PLATO could continue to monitor the same field or move to a Northern field, LOPN1, overlapping with the Kepler field. We assess these two observing strategies in terms of sensitivity of detecting small and long period planets, and subsequently we estimate yields for both observing strategies. Within this we account for including the Kepler data in the LOPN1 field, monitoring of up to 8 years of the LOPS2 field, and the sensitivity improvement from combining TESS and PLATO data since both fields have been and will be continued to be observed with TESS. We find that by monitoring both fields, and hence more stars, the total number of newly discovered planets is maximised. However, to optimise the number of discovered small and long periods planets, long-term monitoring of one field produces the best results. The combination of Kepler and PLATO data improves detection sensitivities; however, as this only covers a fraction of the LOPN1 field and stellar sample, it improves the yields of small and long period planets only marginally. Hence, to detect and characterise small and long period planets in this sparsely explored exoplanet parameter space, a longer continuous observation of the LOPS2 field is best.

## 63. [Scaling Up: A Global Optical/X-ray Correlation For Reprocessing Across Black Hole Masses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17248) <a id="link63"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17248

**Authors:** Changhyun Cho, David M. Russell, Aaryan Sharma, Payaswini Saikia, Maria Cristina Baglio, Kevin Alabarta, Sandeep K. Rout, Yi-Jung Yang, Eleonora Caruso, Mokhine Motsoaledi, Meriana Allah, Ian M. McHardy

**Abstract:** This study extends the optical/X-ray correlation from black hole low-mass X-ray binaries (XRBs) to active galactic nuclei (AGN), bridging scales from stellar-mass to supermassive black holes (SMBHs). In XRBs, optical emission predominantly originates from the thermal reprocessing of high-energy photons on the surface of the geometrically thin, optically thick accretion disk. The reprocessed optical luminosity follows a power-law relation of the form $L_{V} \propto L_{X}^β a$, with $β\sim 0.5$, where $L_X$ is the X-ray luminosity and the orbital separation $a$ serves as a proxy for the disk size. To test the universality of this relationship across all mass scales, we analyzed quasi-simultaneous X-ray and optical flux data from a sample of XRBs and AGN that have estimates of the radius of the optically emitting disk. To bridge the structural differences between these populations, we utilized the characteristic optical/X-ray time lag derived from reverberation mapping as a physical proxy for the reprocessing radius. Our analysis reveals a tight, global correlation across all black hole masses, described by $\log L_V = 0.48\log L_X + 0.96\log a_R + 6.5$ with a scatter of $\sim 0.20$ dex, where $a_R$ is the reprocessing radius derived from the lag. Incorporating a geometric correction for the disk inclination angle ($\cos i$) slightly reduces this scatter to $0.18$ dex. These results suggest that X-ray reprocessing is scale-invariant across accretion disks, from stellar-mass black holes to SMBHs. This scaling relation provides new constraints for models of X-ray emission regions in accreting black holes.

## 64. [Radial increase of the compensated interplanetary magnetic field magnitude as a result of the growth of spherically polarized Alfvén waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15741) <a id="link64"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.15741

**Authors:** Stuart D. Bale, Timothy S. Horbury, Lorenzo Matteini

**Abstract:** Measurements from the NASA Parker Solar Probe (PSP) mission demonstrate that inside $\sim20$ solar radii ($\Rs$) the magnitude of the interplanetary magnetic field does not fall off simply as $1/R^2$, with $\avg{|B|}\,R^2$ 15--20\% below its value at $\sim60\,\Rs$. Here we use PSP magnetic field measurements over $10.6$--$56.8\,\Rs$ to show that the outward rise of $\avg{|B|}\,R^2$ is not due to the global structure of the heliospheric magnetic field or proximity to the heliospheric current sheet but is the direct result of the growth of large-amplitude, spherically polarized Alfvén waves (so-called `switchbacks'). Over the same distance range, $\avg{B_R}\,R^2$ stays constant, so that the sector-signed radial flux is conserved. The wave amplitude $ε= B_{\perp,{\rm rms}}/\avg{B_\parallel}$ grows from $0.26$ to $0.57$ as $R^{1/2}\cosα$, and $\avg{|B|}/\avg{B_R}$ rises by $20\%$. A simple model parametrized by the broadening of the distribution of deflection angles reproduces the effect to high accuracy. These results demonstrate that as the relative wave amplitudes grow with increasing solar distance, a dynamo-like process operates to enhance the field magnitude by up to $20\%$. By enhancing magnetic pressure this has implications for the large scale solar wind structure in the low-beta inner heliosphere, as well as partially resolving the long-standing discrepancy between remote measurements of the solar magnetic field and those made in situ by earlier missions farther from the Sun.

We followed up this pulsar with Murriyang/Parkes radio telescope and obtained a phase-connected timing solution spanning one year, revealing a 4.5-day binary orbit with very low eccentricity (e ~ 1.9 x $10^{-5}$). A follow-up search in LAT data revealed gamma-ray pulsations, allowing for a joint radio and gamma-ray timing analysis. Assuming a neutron-star mass of 1.5 Msun, the inferred median companion mass (~0.2 Msun) is consistent with a helium white dwarf. The pulsar has a low dispersion measure of 17.0 pc cm$^{-3}$ and shows strong scintillation, leading to it being detected in ~72% of the observations covering the 704-1174 MHz band with Murriyang. The pulsar has a narrow duty cycle of ~6.5%, which leads to individual time of arrival (ToA) uncertainties of ~4.5 us for a 10 min integration with MeerKAT, suggesting this pulsar is well suited for inclusion in PTA experiments.

## 66. [A strategy for astrometric quasar selection with Gaia DR3 and its application to discover new luminous $z>2$ quasars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13742) <a id="link66"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.13742

**Authors:** J. Li, J. P. U. Fynbo

**Abstract:** We aim to provide a cookbook for purely astrometric selection of quasars with Gaia DR3 and to provide an example of its use for the identification of very luminous $z>2$ quasars. We analyse the Gaia DR3 astrometry of confirmed quasars and stationary sources. We show that the proper motion signal to noise $μ/σ_μ$ follows a Rayleigh-like distribution. We also show the importance of properly taking into account the correlation between proper motions in right ascension and declination, as well as the non-linear error propagation. We adopt the Mahalanobis distance to the null proper-motion hypothesis. A scaled $χ^2$ distribution with $k=2$ and scale $s=1.1485$ is fitted to Gaia-CRF3; the corresponding $\pm2σ$ Gaussian-equivalent threshold is $D^2_{M(μ)}<7.097$. The final criteria combine this with the parallax condition and $\mathrm{ruwe}<1.4$. Applying the new criteria to the SDSS DR16Q confirmed quasars, the recovery rate is $90.25\%$. The suggested purely astrometric selection significantly improves both completeness and purity compared to other strategies used in the literature, reaching a quasar purity of approximately $95\%$ ($\sim2σ$ Gaussian-equivalent). It can be used to improve the selection purity and completeness for future quasar surveys. As an example, we use the suggested strategi to locate 24 very bright $z>2$ quasars without redshifts in the literature. Our proposed astrometric selection strategy recovers 95\% of known quasars. Its potential use for new discoveries is demonstrated by the identification of 24 very luminous $z>2$ quasars. Ten of these are new discoveries and for an additional seven we correct the redshifts determined by other methods.}

## 67. [Global analysis of tensor mode helicity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12947) <a id="link67"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.12947

**Authors:** Anna C. Tsai, Dylan L. Jow, Ue-Li Pen

**Abstract:** A detection of helicity in a primordial gravitational-wave background (GWB) would provide compelling evidence for parity violation in the early universe and point toward physics beyond the standard picture of inflation. Since big bang cosmology is not time translation invariant, we identify that the so-called ``regular'' and often-neglected ``irregular'' solutions are independent degrees of freedom that can in principle carry different, even opposite, helicities. We note that this possibility complicates the interpretation of a measured net helicity. To determine the sensitivity of various GW detector configurations to helicity, we introduce a universal helicity efficiency coefficient that provides a unified geometric description of helicity sensitivity across gravitational-wave detectors which generalizes and improves previous estimators. We apply this helicity efficiency coefficient to estimate the sensitivity of a range of future GW experiments to helicity across twenty orders of magnitude in frequency assuming a fully helical background with an amplitude just below the current constraints.

## 68. [Quasinormal modes of rotating black holes with ultralight scalar fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16054) <a id="link68"></a>

**Authors:** Leif Lui, Adrian Ka-Wai Chung

**Abstract:** We report the first accurate computation of the leading-order shifts in the quasinormal-mode frequencies of rapidly rotating black holes surrounded by massive scalar fields with nonminimal curvature coupling. For fields with reduced Compton wavelengths no shorter than the black-hole mass scale, the numerical errors in the frequency shifts are typically $10^{-8} - 10^{-5}$ for dimensionless spins between 0 and 0.9, and at most $\mathcal{O}(10^{-3})$ next to the scalar-mass threshold. Our results open up the possibility of searching for ultralight scalar particles, prime dark-matter candidates, using existing gravitational-wave signals.

## 69. [DEMOS. II. What the full Lupus demographics truly tell us about protoplanetary disc evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17284) <a id="link69"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17284

**Authors:** Lorenzo A. Malanga, Giovanni P. Rosotti, Giuseppe Lodato, Alice Somigliana, Benoît Tabone, Rossella Anania, Carlo F. Manara, Leonardo Testi, Claudia Toci, Francesco Zagaria

**Abstract:** Assessing whether disc evolution is driven by viscosity or MHD winds is crucial to determine the conditions in which planets form. The rich statistical information encoded in nearby star-forming regions provides an excellent opportunity to probe disc evolution. In this paper, we apply the Bayesian inference tool DEMOS, designed to exploit the statistical properties of disc populations and infer initial conditions and model parameters, to the Lupus disc population. We consider the observed distributions of stellar mass $M_\star$, disc mass $M$ (from continuum flux), mass accretion rate $\dot{M}$ and CO disc size $R_{90%, CO}$. We constrain all parameters for both the viscous model, with internal photoevaporation, and the MHD winds model, with unprecedented precision. The inferred initial conditions suggest that positive correlations between initial disc properties and $M_\star$ must be present. Both models yield a typical $R_0$ of $3-5$ au for a disc around a $0.3\ \text{M}_\odot$ star, with positive stellar-mass dependence. We infer $α$ (or $α_\text{DW}$) of $3-4 \times 10^{-4}$ for discs around $0.3\ \text{M}_\odot$ stars, with an approximately quadratic dependence on $M_\star$. For the viscous-photoevaporative model, the typical wind mass-loss rate is $\dot{M}_\text{w} \sim 10^{-9}$ M$_\odot$ yr$^{-1}$, scaling with $M_\star$ with an exponent of $\sim 2.5$. The inferred mean age is 1.6 Myr for the MHD winds model and 0.9 Myr for the viscous-photoevaporative model. Despite a slight preference for the latter, Bayesian evidence shows that both models reproduce the data comparably well, and neither can be ruled out. However, their best-fit synthetic populations predict substantially different evolutionary pathways. Applying DEMOS to multiple populations, especially evolved ones like Upper Scorpius, will allow to place stronger constraints on disc evolution mechanisms.

## 70. [Cosmic Colliders in Binary Blazars Probing Ultra-High-Energy Particle Physics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17033) <a id="link70"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17033

**Authors:** Giorgio Galanti

**Abstract:** While terrestrial colliders and fixed-target cosmic-ray interactions are bounded at center-of-mass (CM) energies $\sqrt{s} \lesssim \mathcal{O}(10^5)\,\text{GeV}$, we demonstrate that bound sub-parsec binary blazars form broad-band, beam-beam hadronic ``Cosmic Colliders'', overcoming such limitation. Driven by modest jet misalignments ($θ_{\rm mis} \sim 1^\circ - 6^\circ$), co-streaming proton beams collide at CM energies reaching $\sqrt{s} \sim 10^{10}\,\text{GeV}$ - six orders of magnitude above the Large Hadron Collider. Operating in a strictly optically thin regime ($τ_{pp} \ll 1$), the system performs a continuous CM energy scan, probing asymptotic QCD scaling, gluon saturation, and Froissart unitarity, while opening a possible high-sensitivity window into physics beyond the Standard Model. Secondary PeV-EeV neutrinos and sub-TeV $γ$-rays fall directly within reach of current and planned observatories such as LHAASO, CTAO, IceCube, and GRAND, establishing binary blazars as laboratories for physics at the ultimate energy limit.

## 71. [Ultraviolet Reflected-Light Spectroscopy of Modern Earth Analogs with Starshade Observations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17377) <a id="link71"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17377

**Authors:** Zahra Ahmed, Stuart Shaklan, Mario Damiano, Armen Tokadjian, Edward W. Schwieterman, Simone D'Amico

**Abstract:** Characterizing rocky, habitable zone planets in reflected light is a key science objective for future direct imaging missions, and the ultraviolet (UV) is uniquely suited for supporting the characterization of Earth-like atmospheres. The Hartley-Huggins ozone band at $0.25 ~μ\mathrm{m}$ remains detectable at low spectral resolution down to $0.1\%$ PAL $\mathrm{O}_2$, providing a sensitive proxy for oxygen-bearing chemistry even for Proterozoic-like atmospheres where the visible and near-infrared $\mathrm{O}_2$ features become inaccessible. This work uses an end-to-end framework for UV starshade spectroscopy of modern Earth analogs that integrates high-fidelity observation simulation, conservative post-processing, and Bayesian spectral retrieval to quantify ozone detection significance. The framework is applied across 108 simulated scenes spanning a range of planet phase angles, system inclinations, and exozodiacal dust densities. The results establish that robust ozone detection with UV-only, $R=7$ reflected light spectroscopy requires a photometric $\mathrm{SNR} \sim 30$. UV starshade observations with the Habitable Worlds Observatory achieve this threshold within 3-day integrations for solar-type systems at 10 parsecs with inclinations up to $60^\circ$ at favorable phases ($\leq 90^\circ$). Above this SNR threshold, ozone detections are robust to systematic bias from imperfect exozodi calibration within $\sim30\%$, and the median retrieved ozone abundance remains accurate to within one order of magnitude of the true value.

## 72. [The Cosmic Radio Dipole and Observer Effects in SKA all-sky surveys](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17022) <a id="link72"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17022

**Authors:** D. Bertacca, L. Böhme, B. Bottazzi Baldi, C. Burigana, M. Y. Elkhashab, C. L. Hale, S. von Hausegger, H. R. Klöckner, M. Land-Strykowski, G. F. Lewis, V. Mittal, O. T. Oayda, D. J. Schwarz, P. Tiwari, T. Trombetti, J. D. Wagenveld

**Abstract:** The Square Kilometre Array (SKA) will observe our Universe on the largest scales and so enable the study of fundamental assumptions in cosmology, such as cosmic homogeneity and isotropy. Observations over recent years have reaffirmed an unexpected challenge to the Cosmological Principle first seen in measurements of the cosmic radio dipole. This dipole anisotropy is thought to be caused by our local observer motion, however, it is observed to be about three times larger than predicted, with significances exceeding 5$σ$. The SKA is in a unique position to shed light on this anomaly by measuring this and other effects related to our observer motion. To quantify this, we forecast the capability of large-angle HI and continuum surveys of the SKA to constrain the kinematic matter dipole in galaxy number counts, the modulation of the matter power spectrum by a kinematic dipole, and kinematic spectral distortions of diffuse radio emission.

## 73. [$\texttt{NFDiag}$: High-dimensional MCMC Convergence Diagnosis with Normalizing Flows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16574) <a id="link73"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16574

**Authors:** Kutay Nazlı, Elena Sellentin

**Abstract:** Accurately assessing the convergence of Monte Carlo Markov Chains is crucial for ensuring their validity in Bayesian inference problems. However, convergence assessment in high dimensions is known to be difficult, due to most convergence diagnostics becoming insensitive beyond a few dozen parameters. Conventional convergence statistics analyze marginal distributions which in high dimensions neglects increasing levels of information from the joint and conditional probability distributions. Thus we propose $\texttt{NFDiag}$, which taps into the information of the full multivariate joint posterior with a normalizing flow (NF). Our convergence diagnostic thereby also analyzes information from conditional distributions. In other words, by working in the full multivariate space, it is sensitive with respect to the geometry of the chain in high dimensions. Our diagnostic utilizes that NFs assume independent and identically distributed (iid) training samples, which is a testable assumption that will be violated by a non-converged or strongly correlated chain. Our diagnostic trains NFs on separate parts of a chain, and then assesses whether these correspond to the same multivariate target distribution. We test our diagnostic in up to 250 dimensions, and demonstrate it is universal and unbiased, and detects problematic chain quality also when Gelman-Rubin $\hat{R}$ and the multivarite effective sample size do not.

## 74. [A Quest for Changing-State AGNs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16982) <a id="link74"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16982

**Authors:** Kasra Mokhtarpour, Vicki Sarajedini

**Abstract:** We use Gaia Data Release 3 (DR3) light curves to identify photometrically selected changing-state active galactic nucleus (CSAGN) candidates. We employ unsupervised deep learning and anomaly detection methods to identify unusual patterns of variability in the light curves of 872,228 AGNs with known variability. We find that 8,504 light curves show anomalous behavior. Among these, 682 sources show evidence for two-state photometric behavior during the Gaia DR3 observing baseline. These 682 CSAGN candidates were selected using an automated statistical pattern recognition approach based on Gaussian mixture models (GMMs) to identify sources whose magnitude distributions are consistent with two preferred brightness levels, substantially reducing the need for human visual inspection in selecting the CSAGN candidates. The candidates tend to have fainter absolute magnitudes and lower redshifts than the non-anomalous Gaia variable AGN sample, suggesting that some may be accreting at lower rates. Our approach builds a large candidate sample with less reliance on human visual selection. Follow-up observations of these sources can help identify what drives changing-state transitions and clarify their connection to accretion-disk physics and supermassive black hole (SMBH) fueling.

## 75. [OASIS: On-the-fly ASynchronous Image Stream classification -- Transformer-based models for the early detection of strongly lensed supernovae](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17168) <a id="link75"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17168

**Authors:** Satadru Bag, Raoul Canameras, Sherry H. Suyu, Stefan Schuldt, Stefan Taubenberger, Irham Taufik Andika, Alejandra Melo

**Abstract:** Time-domain surveys often produce asynchronous image sequences across irregular observation times and photometric bands. In many applications, predictions are needed after each new observation, particularly when transient sources must be identified early to trigger follow-up. Strongly lensed supernovae (LSNe), for which follow-up is particularly time-critical, provide a useful example. We formulate this setting as On-the-fly ASynchronous Image Stream (OASIS) classification and explore reusable transformer architectures for causal, on-the-fly classification. We demonstrate these architectures on the lensed-transient classification problem studied by Bag et al. (2026a,b), distinguishing LSNe Ia from unlensed astrophysical transients. We compare them with the recurrent baseline adopted by Bag et al. (2026b), which combines a ConvLSTM branch for image processing with a separate LSTM branch for encoding observation times. The baseline maps asynchronous observations onto a fixed band-by-observation grid, filling unobserved bands with zeros. Using a common training setup, we compare two transformer-based approaches to modeling interactions among spatio-temporal tokens: factorized attention, which separates spatial and temporal interactions, and joint attention, which models them together. On the demonstration dataset, factorized architectures achieve mean losses of $0.095$--$0.100$, compared with $0.128$ for the recurrent baseline and $0.107$ or higher for joint architectures. A selective readout further reduces the best loss to $0.089$ for a factorized model. These results suggest that factorized attention suits this dataset better, without implying universal architectural superiority. We release the architectures as reusable foundations for time-domain astronomy and other applications involving evolving spatial features that require on-the-fly predictions.

## 76. [Unifying spherical convolutions through lifting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13892) <a id="link76"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.13892

**Authors:** J. D. McEwen

**Abstract:** We develop a unified description of convolution on the sphere by lifting to the rotation group. A number of definitions of spherical convolution have been proposed, differing in whether the kernel is axisymmetric or directional, whether the output is on the sphere or the rotation group, whether the signals carry spin, and in their computational cost. No unified treatment exists. Regarding the sphere as the homogeneous space $S^2 \cong \text{SO(3)}/\text{SO(2)}$, we lift a spin $s$ field to the rotation group, convolve on $\text{SO(3)}$, and project back to the sphere through the adjoint of the lift for a potentially different spin $s'$. We recover every rotation-equivariant convolution considered previously through constraints on the kernel, spin choices and inclusion or omission of the lift and projection. For maps between fields of fixed input and output spin, we prove that directional kernels, output on the sphere and equivariance cannot be achieved simultaneously. We make explicit that spin fields are sections of associated line bundles, linking the physics and bundle literatures, and express the spin rotation phase as the character of the twist of the section. We present harmonic-space algorithms for the exact and efficient computation of lifted spherical convolutions of band-limited fields by appealing to sampling theorems on the sphere and rotation group. All operators are implemented in the open-source s2conv code, and numerical experiments confirm the reductions and the equivariance of the operators to machine precision. Finally, we illustrate the operators on fields measured on the Earth and on the celestial sky.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 5

## 77. [A dust-reddened quasar in a [C II]-luminous host at z=6.55](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13758) <a id="link77"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.13758

**Authors:** Daming Yang, Joseph F. Hennawi, Rychard Bouwens, Timo Kist, Eduardo Bañados, Hiddo S. B. Algera, Aaron J. Barth, Sarah E. I. Bosman, Frederick B. Davies, Anna-Christina Eilers, Xiaohui Fan, Victoria A. Fawcett, Yuming Fu, Anniek J. Gloudemans, Francesco Guarneri, Bin Jia, Elia Pizzati, Huub Röttgering, Jan-Torge Schindler, Feige Wang, Jinyi Yang

**Abstract:** Most known quasars at $z>6$ were selected through their rest-frame UV emission. Only a small number of reddened quasars have been identified at these redshifts, leaving their nuclear and host-galaxy properties poorly constrained. We present JWST/NIRSpec spectroscopy and ALMA band 6 observations of the quasar J1440+0019 at $z=6.5488$, revealing its dust-reddened nature and jointly characterizing its nucleus and host galaxy. The continuum is best described by an SMC-like extinction law with $E(B-V)=0.20\pm0.03$. Three absorption systems detected in N V, Si IV, and C IV are blueshifted by approximately $900$--$3600$ km s$^{-1}$ and likely trace an ionized nuclear outflow. The broad Mg II line and extinction-corrected continuum yield a black-hole mass of $\log(M_{\rm BH}/M_\odot)=9.04\pm0.42$, a bolometric luminosity of $L_{\rm bol}\simeq1.1\times10^{47}$ erg s$^{-1}$, and an Eddington ratio of $λ_{\rm Edd}\simeq0.8$. ALMA observations further reveal its host galaxy to be among the most [C II]-luminous known at $z>6$, with $L_{\mathrm{[C II]}}=(7.21\pm0.25)\times10^9 L_\odot$. For a fiducial dust temperature of 47 K and emissivity index $β=1.6$, the dust continuum implies $L_{\rm TIR}=8.9\times10^{12} L_\odot$ and $M_{\rm dust}=4.0\times10^8 M_\odot$. The inferred dynamical mass places J1440+0019 in the region of the $M_{\rm BH}$--$M_{\rm dyn}$ plane occupied by high-redshift blue quasars. These observations reveal a moderately reddened, rapidly accreting quasar in a luminous, dust-rich host during reionization. Its measured nuclear and host-galaxy properties provide an observational reference for identifying and characterizing similar systems in future systematic searches with wide-area infrared surveys.

## 78. [Flip-Flop Shocks and Tori in Bondi-Hoyle-Lyttleton Accretion onto Rotating Kerr-Weyl Black Holes and Their Quasi-Periodic Variability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13384) <a id="link78"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.13384

**Authors:** Orhan Donmez, Imtiaz Khan, G. Mustafa

**Abstract:** We present GRHD simulations of Bondi-Hoyle-Lyttleton accretion onto a rotating black hole in Weyl conformal gravity, described by the Kerr-Weyl metric. The equatorial flow is evolved for spins a=0.9M and a=0.5M and several Weyl coefficients, which enter through an effective curvature parameter k, and is compared with Kerr runs sharing the same setup. For the k considered, the horizon, ISCO and test-particle frequencies differ from Kerr by at most ~2%. However, the curvature term grows as kr^2 and acts at the accretion radius R_acc. The flow is controlled by one quantity, eta=|k|R_acc^3/M, the ratio of curvature-induced to Newtonian acceleration at R_acc. For eta<~0.03 a stable shock cone forms, indistinguishable from Kerr. For eta~0.2-0.3 the anti-de Sitter-like term traps shocked gas behind the black hole and drives persistent flip-flop oscillations. For eta>~1.6 the cone is destroyed and an oscillating torus forms. Spin modifies only the inner few gravitational radii and does not change the regime. In strongly deviating models the mean accretion rate is 1.7-3 times the Kerr value and varies by 20-60% rms, versus 0.1-1% in Kerr-like flows. The power spectra show global QPOs with fundamentals at 2.9-4.8 Hz for a 10 Msun black hole, with harmonic ladders whose 3:2 and 2:1 ratios come from one non-sinusoidal oscillation, not resonances. These lie in the low-frequency QPO band and are unrelated to test-particle frequencies (~70-200 Hz). They scale as f_0=xi f_K(R_acc), with xi~0.7-1.1 for stable cones and xi~0.23-0.38 for flip-flop and torus regimes. Strong oscillations would be detectable within seconds for a bright Galactic source. The required |k| exceeds galactic-rotation-curve values by 18-36 orders of magnitude, so a detected shock-driven QPO would measure a local deviation from Kerr, and its absence would constrain |k|.

## 79. [Impact of ab initio E1 strength on neutron capture rates along the nickel isotopic chain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13868) <a id="link79"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.13868

**Authors:** Francesca Bonaiti, Joanna E. Sobczyk

**Abstract:** Nucleosynthesis simulations of the r-process require neutron capture rates for neutron-rich nuclei, many of which lie beyond experimental reach. Predicting these rates relies on theoretical calculations of the low-energy electric dipole (E1) strength near the neutron threshold. We present the first ab initio computation of the E1 strength along the nickel isotopic chain, for $^{58}$Ni and the even-even isotopes $^{68-80}$Ni. The E1 strength is obtained with the Lorentz integral transform coupled-cluster (LIT-CC) method, extended to axially symmetric reference states to reach open-shell nuclei, using two- and three-nucleon interactions from chiral effective field theory. The approach is benchmarked against the measured photoabsorption cross section of $^{58}$Ni and the E1 strength of $^{68}$Ni. Along the chain we find low-lying E1 strength above the neutron threshold that grows with neutron number. Including it in the reaction code TALYS enhances the neutron capture rates forming $^{68-78}$Ni between 3 and 10 GK, with an effect that increases with temperature and neutron number and an uncertainty dominated by the inversion of the integral transform. For $^{80}$Ni, where strong pygmy dipole strength lies just above threshold, the enhancement sets in already around 0.5 GK. Together, these results open the way to ab initio input for neutron capture rates along isotopic chains far from stability.

## 80. [Binary Ellis-Bronnikov wormholes in 3+1 numerical relativity: mergers, fly-bys and gravitational waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13206) <a id="link80"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.13206

**Authors:** Nikita M. Shirokov, Ilya Nachevsky

**Abstract:** We present what are, to our knowledge, the first three-dimensional numerical-relativity evolutions of wormhole binaries: pairs of massive Ellis-Bronnikov wormholes held open by a phantom scalar field, whose energy density is nowhere positive. We construct constraint-satisfying initial data for single throats and for pairs, at rest and with orbital momentum, and evolve them with adaptive mesh refinement. An isolated throat is unstable: depending on the sign of a small perturbation, it collapses to a black hole or inflates, at the rate that linear perturbation theory predicts to within 1.9%. Two throats at rest repel when their scalar charges have the same sign and attract when the signs are opposite, with a strength ratio close to the scalar force law's prediction, so only opposite-signed pairs merge. A head-on collision forms a common horizon around both throats while they are still open. The horizon then loses 28% of its area as it absorbs the scalar field, and approaches the Schwarzschild radius of the pair's mass. Pairs that fly past each other form no horizon, and both throats inflate. Every encounter emits a short burst of gravitational waves and a scalar dipole wave of comparable, negative energy. An isolated collapsing throat rings at the quasinormal frequency of the black hole it becomes. No throat survives more than a few orbital periods, even when its initial perturbation is tiny, so these binaries do not inspiral. The results hold for one phantom-scalar model, equal masses, a single throat compactness and no spin.

## 81. [Stellar discs and intermediate-mass black holes in galactic nuclei II. Fragmenting the disc and forming the S-stars in a live nuclear star cluster](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14179) <a id="link81"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.14179

**Authors:** Taras Panamarev, Bence Kocsis

**Abstract:** We present direct $N$-body simulations of a stellar disc around the supermassive black hole Sgr~A*, perturbed by an intermediate-mass black hole (IMBH) and embedded in a live nuclear star cluster. A retrograde IMBH with $m_\bullet=2000\,M_\odot$, relative inclination $θ_\bullet=150^\circ$, and initial semi-major axis $a_\bullet=0.15$~pc breaks the disc into three disc-like components and an angular momentum filament. The stochastic torques of the cluster, together with the IMBH's inward migration, increase the angular separation between these substructures. By ${\sim}5$~Myr, their separations become comparable to those among the observed Galactic-centre young star structures. The innermost ($a\le0.04$~pc) disc stars evolve into an S-star-like population. Relaxation driven by the cluster disperses their orbital planes, excites their eccentricities and depletes the `zone of avoidance' within ${\sim}10$~Myr even without an IMBH. The IMBH further accelerates their orbital-plane reorientation. However, the most eccentric S-stars may require an additional formation channel, such as the Hills mechanism. The distinctive signatures of the IMBH are the well-separated disc components and the eccentricity excitation confined to the radial range swept by its orbit. Dynamical friction from the cluster reduces the orbital size of a $2000\,M_\odot$ IMBH initially at $0.15$~pc by more than a factor of two within $10$~Myr. Prograde IMBHs tend to align with the stellar disc, whereas a $2000\,M_\odot$ retrograde IMBH can anti-align with the radially overlapping disc stars. We also quantify how the IMBH affects the supply of stars to the loss cone and the rates of stellar tidal disruptions and binary separations.

## 82. [Gravitational waves from strong first-order phase transitions in an expanding Universe](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14806) <a id="link82"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.14806

**Authors:** Jintao Zou, Zizhuo Zhao, Yongtao Jia, Ligong Bian

**Abstract:** We investigate the gravitational waves generated by first-order phase transitions (PTs) in an expanding Universe. Using a scalar field-fluid coupled model, we perform three-dimensional lattice numerical simulations of slow first-order PTs with strong strength. We observe that the contribution from sound waves to the gravitational wave (GW) signal significantly dominates over that from bubble collisions. The sound wave spectra exhibit two peaks located at the mean bubble separation and the sound-shell width. Rotational modes are generated in the fluid during the bubble collisions, and their magnitude grows with the PT strength. The lifetime of the sound wave source increases with decreasing $α$: for weaker transitions the source can persist for longer than a Hubble time, while for stronger transitions it decays within a Hubble time. When the walls propagate as deflagrations, the GW spectral shape changes drastically, with steeper ultraviolet tails and a shorter-lived sound wave source. We also observe that the magnitude of the normalized GWs is roughly an order of magnitude smaller than obtained in the Minkowski spacetime simulations.

## 83. [DEMOS I. A Bayesian inference framework to constrain disc evolution from observed demographics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17254) <a id="link83"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17254

**Authors:** Lorenzo A. Malanga, Giovanni P. Rosotti, Giuseppe Lodato, Benoît Tabone, Alice Somigliana, Leonardo Testi

**Abstract:** The mechanism driving the evolution and dispersal of protoplanetary discs has a substantial impact on the theories of planet formation. To date, neither the viscous paradigm nor the MHD wind-driven framework have been ruled out by the observations. The statistical richness of the observed samples provides a unique opportunity to test evolutionary models through population synthesis. However, comparisons between models and observations are normally carried out by eye, and a more statistically rigorous approach has not yet been implemented. We present DEMOS, a Bayesian inference tool that aims to infer the model parameters and initial conditions that best reproduce the full distribution of global properties of disc populations. This framework relies on a comparison between observations and synthetic populations generated with the 1D disc population-synthesis code Diskpop. We tested the tool with both the MHD wind model and the viscous framework with internal photoevaporation. Applying DEMOS to synthetic populations, we find that, for both models, it accurately recovers the model parameters of disc populations and their associated uncertainties, including cases in which initial conditions are correlated with the stellar mass. In the optimal scenario in which disc masses, accretion rates and disc sizes are available, the tool can also constrain the angular-momentum transport parameter. We show that, although parameter estimates are the most precise with samples of about 100 discs, the tool also yields meaningful constraints for smaller populations of 10-30 discs. We further find that the predictions obtained with DEMOS closely align with those found by previous works. We anticipate that DEMOS will be a valuable tool to infer the initial conditions of observed disc populations, to design observational programmes, and to assess which evolutionary model best reproduces the data.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 5

## 84. [Sensitivity of calibration-free DESI DR2 BAO--supernova constraints to a distance-duality tilt](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16496) <a id="link84"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16496

**Authors:** Mikel Artola, Stéphane Fay, Leandros Perivolaropoulos

**Abstract:** Recent measurements from DESI baryon acoustic oscillations (BAO) have reported a moderate preference for dynamical dark energy, challenging the $Λ$CDM paradigm. Nonetheless, such a trend could reflect a tension between the shape and the offset of the Hubble diagrams predicted by current BAO and Type Ia supernovae (SN) data, which may underpin a possible breakdown of the cosmic distance duality relation linking luminosity distances $D_{\rm L}$ and angular-diameter distances $D_{\rm A}$. We explore this possibility by parametrizing deviations from this relation as $η(z)\equiv D_{\rm L}/[{(1+z)}^2D_{\rm A}]=α{(1+z)}^\varepsilon$, and investigate how the preference for a Chevallier-Polarski-Linder (CPL) dynamical dark energy is modified once $\varepsilon\neq0$ is allowed. Using DESI DR2 and uncalibrated SN data (Pantheon$+$, DES-Dovekie, Union3, and Union3.1), we find that allowing $\varepsilon$ to vary freely in both $Λ$CDM and CPL reduces the CPL preference with respect to $Λ$CDM by a larger amount than the addition of spatial curvature $Ω_k$; only Union3.1 exhibits comparable reductions. We also find that the preference reduction is robust to alternative $η(z)$ parametrizations, and that the $η(z)$ constraints remain stable under the removal of BAO tracers or low-redshift cutoffs for Pantheon$+$, whereas the DES-Dovekie preference largely disappears once $z<0.1$ SN are removed. In a separate calibrated analysis, illustrative Planck sound-horizon and SH0ES absolute-magnitude priors give $α\simeq0.942\pm0.014$, quantifying the normalization mismatch under those assumptions. We also discuss photon-loss and varying-$G$ interpretations: ordinary positive opacity cannot explain the negative central tilts $\varepsilon$ found for a $Λ$CDM background, while local gravity tests strongly constrain the illustrative unscreened varying-$G$ scenario.

## 85. [Cosmology & Astrophysics of the Dark Dimension with Bulk Majorana Neutrinos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15209) <a id="link85"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.15209

**Authors:** Luis A. Anchordoqui, Cumrun Vafa

**Abstract:** Recently, the possibility of having sterile Majorana neutrinos charged under $B-L$ gauge symmetry in the bulk of the dark dimension has been explored. We point out a cosmological restriction on the Majorana mass of bulk neutrinos leading to mass $\gtrsim 100~{\rm GeV}$. This avoids the over-production of Kaluza-Klein neutrino towers in the early universe thus resolving the cosmological neutrino catastrophe of extra dimension scenarios. If the mass of the bulk neutrino is at the lower end of this bound, it can potentially also explain the recently observed Fermi halo excess on gamma rays through the evaporation of long-lived 5d (i.e. dark) black holes through Hawking radiation to bulk neutrinos, which promptly decay into standard model particles.

Petrucci, N. Pourré, S. Rabien, D. C. Ribeiro, S. Robbe-Dubois, M. Sadun Bordoni, J. Sanchez-Bermudez, J. Sauter, M. Scialpi, J. Scigliuto, J. Shangguan, T. Shimizu, F. Soulez, C. Straubmeier, E. **Abstract:** We present a systematic interferometric search for close supermassive black hole (SMBH) binaries in 20 nearby type 1 active galactic nuclei (AGN) observed with VLTI/\grav. We investigate parsec-scale asymmetric emission by modeling the squared visibilities and closure phases with a single circular Gaussian component plus a background point source for the central AGN, and searching for additional compact companions through grid-based binary models. The robustness of the search is validated using injection-recovery simulations and point-spread-function diagnostics. Several features are identified in the initial significance maps, but only one candidate out of 20 shows a persistent compact asymmetry, corresponding to an observed incidence of $5\%$. If NGC~3783 is interpreted as a binary AGN, we derive a Poissonian one-sided $1σ$ upper limit of $f_{\rm bin}<16.5\%$ for GRAVITY-detectable binaries among bright nearby AGN (K<12.5) at separations of $\sim1$-$100$~pc. Conversely, if the observed asymmetry in NGC~3783 is associated with asymmetric circumnuclear dust emission, the corresponding upper limit on the binary fraction is $f_{\rm bin}<9.2\%$. We compare these results with predictions from the Horizon-AGN simulation, finding comparable binary fractions. We also noted that the expected fraction strongly depends on the assumed distribution of individual SMBH accretion rates within binaries, which is currently unresolved in cosmological models. Our results demonstrate the potential of infrared interferometry to identify parsec-scale binary AGN candidates and motivate future GRAVITY+ observations to expand the sample and follow up known candidates, helping to confirm their nature and distinguish genuine binaries from asymmetric nuclear structures.

## 87. [Collider and direct detection as key probes of asymmetric dark matter: Signals from a compressed Inert Higgs doublet](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14178) <a id="link87"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.14178

**Authors:** David Cerdeño, Miguel Escudero, Pietro Ghedini, Guilherme Guedes, Thomas Hambye, Abel Muñoz-Navarro, Elena Pressendo

**Abstract:** The cosmic imbalance of particles over antiparticles may be inherited by the dark matter in a certain class of asymmetric dark matter models. In this article, we thoroughly investigate the signatures of the minimal realization of this idea. In this model a new (stable) inert Higgs, $H'$, plays the role of the mediator that facilitates the asymmetry transfer from the visible to the dark sector. It also constitutes a small dark matter sub-component, while the dominant component stems from a singlet scalar asymmetry. A combination of phenomenological and cosmological constraints requires this new inert Higgs doublet to be quite light $46\,{\rm GeV}<m_{H'}< 580\,{\rm GeV}$ and its spectrum rather compressed, $ΔM = m_{H^\pm}-m_{H_0} \lesssim 1\,{\rm GeV}$ and $δm = m_{A_0}-m_{H_0}\lesssim 5\,{\rm MeV}$. Therefore its charged component can be relatively long-lived on collider length-scales. By recasting searches for compressed Higgsinos at the LHC, we derive new limits on this particle and show that, while at present most of the parameter space for this state is still unconstrained, large and key regions of parameter space will be tested at the HL-LHC by a combination of searches for disappearing charged tracks and displaced soft tracks. Quite remarkably, the lifetime of the charged component of the doublet can be correlated with the dark matter interaction rate at direct detection experiments and we show that the next generation of direct detection experiments has a unique sensitivity to the parameter space in the model. The fact that a small neutral mass splitting is a prediction of the asymmetric setup makes it an interesting candidate to account for the high energy nuclear recoil event recently reported by the LZ experiment. More generally, our detailed phenomenological study holds for any dark matter model involving an inert Higgs doublet with mass below 580 GeV.

## 88. [An upper limit on Galactic OB+black hole binaries from SDSS-V](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17253) <a id="link88"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17253

**Authors:** J. Müller-Horn, H. -W. Rix, S. Shahaf, B. Pennell, E. Zari, M. C. Ramírez-Tannus, A. Farah, R. Essick, J. I. Villaseñor, M. R. Blanton, J. G. Fernández-Trincado, J. A. Kollmeier, C. Lam, K. Maltsev, A. Roman-Lopes, D. P. Schneider, A. Tkachenko

**Abstract:** Binaries composed of a luminous OB star and a black hole (BH) are a key evolutionary link between massive stellar binaries and merging compact-object binaries, but only a handful are known and their occurrence fraction remains poorly constrained. We use multi-epoch spectroscopy from the SDSS-V Milky Way Mapper to place a first rigorous empirical upper limit on the fraction of Galactic massive ($\geq 10\,\mathrm{M}_\odot$) OB stars with a close BH companion, $f_\mathrm{BH}$. Radial-velocity (RV) variability alone cannot distinguish BH companions from the far more numerous luminous binaries. We therefore combine the SDSS-V RV variability measurements of 3130 massive OB stars with high-resolution follow-up and literature information to identify luminous companions among the RV-variable subset. Forward-modelling the detectability of OB+BH systems using BH masses and orbital parameters from binary-evolution models, we find $f_\mathrm{BH} \lesssim 3\%$ at 95% credibility. This empirical upper limit approaches, but does not exclude, theoretical predictions for the OB+BH fraction. The constraint is primarily limited by the number of RV-variable systems that can be decisively classified, highlighting the importance of complete, high-quality follow-up for future searches.

## 89. [Resistive alignment of a black-hole magnetosphere](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13288) <a id="link89"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.13288

**Authors:** Ruben Lier, Oliver Porth

**Abstract:** Recent numerical simulations have shown that black-hole magnetospheres evolve rapidly toward alignment between the magnetic axis and spin axis but the physical origin of this process has remained unclear. We develop an analytic theory of this alignment within resistive relativistic magnetohydrodynamics. Taking the slow-spin Blandford-Znajek monopole solution as the exterior magnetic field, we reduce the induction equation to a relaxation equation for the current-sheet inclination angle. Our analysis shows that alignment results from second-order spin effects in the Blandford-Znajek solution combined with explicit resistive dissipation. We test this prediction using resistive-GRMHD simulations at several spins and resistivities and compare the measured late-time secular decay with the analytic prediction. This alignment timescale hence carries a unique signature which combines plasma dissipation with spacetime rotation.

## 90. [Equation of State Classification of Core-Collapse Supernovae with a Network of Gravitational-Wave Detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16130) <a id="link90"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16130

**Authors:** A. Akhmetali, Y. S. Abylkairov, J. A. Font, M. Zanolin, E. Abdikamalov

**Abstract:** Gravitational waves from core-collapse supernovae may provide a unique probe of the equation of state (EOS) of dense nuclear matter. In previous work, we assessed the potential of machine-learning methods for EOS classification for rapidly rotating supernovae using individual gravitational-wave detectors. Here, we extend the framework to detector networks and investigate the impact of detector configuration, source distance, arrival time, and sky location on the classification. Using convolutional neural networks, we perform 10-class EOS classification directly from noisy detector data for both second- and third-generation observatories. We find that detector geometry strongly influences classification performance. For Galactic events, current detector networks can achieve promising classification performance, but the reach depends sensitively on the detector network antenna response. Adding detectors improves time-averaged performance and produces more uniform classification across the sky. Third-generation observatories extend the classification reach to 100 kpc for favorable source orientations but do not remove the dependence on detector geometry.

## 91. [A Comprehensive Interpretation of the LZ High-Energy Recoil Event: A Minimal $U(1)_B$ Dark Sector and Its Experimental Tests](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14188) <a id="link91"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.14188

**Authors:** Mattia Di Mauro

**Abstract:** The $248$ keV nuclear-recoil candidate reported by LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) motivates an inelastic dark matter (DM) interpretation, in which scattering converts the DM particle into a slightly heavier state. We provide a comprehensive study of this interpretation in an anomaly-free model of gauged baryon number, where a new vector boson, $Z_B$, mediates interactions between DM and quarks. Breaking the baryonic gauge symmetry generates the new particle masses and a small splitting between two DM states, while a residual symmetry keeps the lighter state stable. We identify regions compatible with the LZ candidate, the observed DM abundance, and current ATLAS and CMS dijet constraints. Within the scanned domain, these regions span DM masses $m_χ\simeq0.19$-$4.5$ TeV, mediator masses $M_{Z_B}\simeq0.05$-$5.0$ TeV, and gauge couplings $g_B\simeq0.004$-$0.74$, with correlated values of these parameters. A representative thermal solution has $m_χ=1$ TeV, $M_{Z_B}=1.44$ TeV, $g_B\simeq0.228$, and a mass splitting of $300$ keV. A small electroweak mixing allows the heavier DM state to decay rapidly, suppressing unwanted signals from its surviving population. This interpretation can be tested through complementary measurements: additional LZ and XENONnT data can establish whether the high-energy recoil signal persists, ATLAS and CMS Run-3 dijet searches can probe the mediator mass and coupling, and a dedicated IceCube analysis of four-quark final states can test DM capture and annihilation in the Sun. Together, these measurements can test complementary parts of the surviving region and assess whether the LZ candidate is consistent with this interpretation.

## 92. [Magnetar X-ray flares as elementary reconnection events in a self-organized critical magnetosphere](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14581) <a id="link92"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.14581

**Authors:** Sheng-Lun Xie, Yi Zhao, Wen-Long Zhang, Chen-Wei Wang, Yun-Wei Yu, Shao-Lin Xiong

**Abstract:** Magnetar bursts are commonly attributed to magnetic energy release, yet the physically relevant dissipation unit has remained uncertain. Here we argue that this unit is not the burst as conventionally defined, but the short substructures within bursts, namely magnetar X-ray flares (MXFs). Using 789 MXFs from SGR~J1935+2154, we show that their waiting times separate into two components, interpreted as reflecting clustered intra-episode events and longer inter-episode intervals, that their proxy emission measure and temperature exhibit distinct rising and declining trends, and that their energy release scale (ERS) proxy distribution has a high-end slope in the same statistical regime as solar and stellar flares. Combined with the previously measured MXF duration slope, these results identify MXFs as a three-dimensional fractal-diffusive self-organized critical (FD-SOC) system with $β_{\rm diff}\simeq2$, implying reconnection avalanches that expand linearly through an active magnetosphere. We infer that magnetar active episodes are global unloading states assembled from many elementary flares. Magnetar X-ray flares are therefore the physically relevant units for magnetar outburst statistics and for connecting magnetar high-energy activity to other impulsive phenomena.

**Novelty:** 5

## 93. [Aditya-L1 and SDO Observations of a Confined X-Class Flare with an EUV Late Phase: Thermal and Nonthermal Emission, Magnetic Topology, and Flux Rope Confinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16551) <a id="link93"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16551

**Authors:** Vishwa Vijay Singh, Bhuwan Joshi, N. P. S. Mithun

**Abstract:** We study a long-duration, confined X2.0-class solar flare exhibiting three distinct episodes of HXR emission, along with an accompanying EUV late phase. A key highlight of this work is the novel observations from the high-resolution solar X-ray spectrometers SoLEXS and HEL1OS onboard Aditya-L1, providing insights into the evolution of thermal and non-thermal emissions. Simultaneous EUV observations from AIA/SDO in hot channels, combined with multi-band X-ray light curves, reveal a two-stage confined eruption of a large MFR, separated by $\sim$10~minutes. The first stage corresponds to an M4.6 flare, while the overall event reaches X2.0 on the GOES scale. During the first phase, magnetic reconnection beneath the large EUV hot channel causes plasma heating to $\sim$19~MK, with a non-thermal spectral index of $\sim$4.3. In the second phase, characterized by external reconnection and confinement of the MFR hot channel, the temperature increases to $\sim$22~MK, accompanied by further hardening of the spectral index to $\sim$3.9. Despite pronounced non-thermal signatures and enhanced particle acceleration, the MFR fails to erupt through the overlying coronal loops because of the strong strapping force exerted by the large overlying coronal loops. Importantly, the stalled eruption, together with reconnection in the higher overlying loops, results in enhanced heating within a closed magnetic configuration, with temperatures peaking at up to $\sim$15~MK during the late phase. The paper also provides the first observations of detectable non-thermal X-ray emission during the early EUV late phase, albeit briefly, providing direct evidence for reconnection-induced heating, likely occurring in the higher overlying coronal loops.

**Abstract:** The decay of dark matter (DM) is a generic prediction of many particle physics models, producing distinctive monochromatic signatures in environments such as the Galactic halo. Searches for decaying DM in the elusive 'MeV gap' have remained conspicuously under-explored, despite well-motivated models including axion-like particles, sterile neutrinos, and hidden-sector scenarios predicting signatures in this energy range. The upcoming Compton Spectrometer and Imager (COSI) telescope will help bridge this gap. Designed to probe this energy band with high spectral resolution, low background, and wide field of view, COSI is well-suited for DM line searches at the MeV scale. In this work, we make projections for COSI's 2-year sensitivity for DM decay in the Milky Way halo using dedicated all-sky simulations incorporating COSI's predicted instrument response. We conclude that COSI will improve the sensitivity to the DM lifetime by more than an order of magnitude over the DM mass range 0.4 to 10 MeV.

## 95. [Scaled three-dimensional simulations of giant-planet accretion and disk clearing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15876) <a id="link95"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.15876

**Authors:** Ignacio Mosqueira

**Abstract:** We develop accelerated three-dimensional simulations of growing planet pairs that draw on a finite gas reservoir. An annulus compression (Scaling A) shortens the predicted clearing time and shrinks the computational domain at equal grid resolution while preserving key physical parameters. Planetary seeds start with Hill radii equal to the disk scale height, retaining three-dimensional accretion. We demonstrate the method in scaled PDS 70 and Solar-System configurations, assuming perfect Hill capture by growing planets on circular orbits. We compare Scaling A at radius-compression factor \(Γ=1.75\) with the unscaled PDS system at matched resolution, through a 1\% reduction of the interplanetary gas inventory. The scaled run is nearly an order of magnitude faster, with similar accreted mass and a comparable partition of gas between the planets. For the Solar-System configuration at \(Γ=3\), the scaling forecasts a speedup of nearly two orders of magnitude at equal local resolution. The scaling is complementary to orbital advection. On eight CPU cores of a desktop computer, the scaled PDS 70 and Solar-System runs reduce the gas inventory between the orbiting planets by roughly half in less than a day. Accretion-driven orbital convergence narrows the shared feeding zone and, together with gas clearing, limits the planetary growth budget. In our scaled PDS 70 and Solar-System runs, the angular momentum carried by meridional inflow directly constrains delivery theory. These results establish a practical route to testing accretion theory against simulations at matched scaled parameters, before applying it to unscaled planetary systems.

## 96. [Magnetic connectivity from the Sun to the Earth with MHD models II. Impact of input magnetic maps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13620) <a id="link96"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.13620

**Authors:** Barbara Perri, Silke Kennis, Brigitte Schmieder, Stefaan Poedts

**Abstract:** Connectivity estimation needs to be fast and thus usually relies on semi-empirical models. We previously established that optimised magnetohydrodynamics (MHD) models can also be used to derive magnetic connectivity, yielding good results, especially for complex configurations. However, the modelling is not the only factor that can impact the connectivity estimation. Models' results, both semi-empirical and MHD, depend heavily on the input data used to drive the simulation. In order to quantify this impact on our connectivity estimation, we used 20 different maps by four providers. We selected the period of July 2019, which corresponds to Carrington rotation (CR) number 2219, because it had already been studied in a previous paper. It corresponds to a period of minimum activity, meaning minimal interference from transient structures, but with a high-speed stream and several polarity switches. We have compared our results with in situ data from ACE and developed several visualisation techniques to validate connectivity not only for a specific date but also for a full CR. The choice of the input magnetic map drastically affects the connectivity estimates, with global scores going from 28% global agreement to 63% in the most extreme case. The HMI and GONG maps consistently gave the best connectivity estimations, with more than 50% correct results of the full CR. For this specific period, the GONG-ADAPT maps yielded the least reliable results, with a score below 35%. This is not surprising, as we have shown before that COCONUT required parameter adjustments for these maps. However, WSO maps which showed poor coronal agreement actually provided good connectivity estimates, showing that the Heliospheric Current Sheet (HCS) location is in the end the most crucial parameter for connectivity. Consistent data over a full Carrington rotation are to be favored compared to daily.

## 97. [Radiation-Regulated Spherical Accretion with an Inner Accretion Disk](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16097) <a id="link97"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16097

**Authors:** Cheng-Liang Jiao, Liying Zhu, Xiang-dong Shi, Er-gang Zhao

**Abstract:** We develop a theoretical model for radiation-regulated spherical accretion with an inner accretion disk. In our model, the outer accretion flow remains quasi-spherical and optically thick, while the luminosity generated by the inner disk is self-consistently coupled to the accretion rate and irradiates the inflowing gas. By solving the global transonic structure with radiative transfer treated in the diffusion approximation, we investigate how radiation regulates the flow structure and the accretion rate. We find that the luminosity is constrained by a generalized Eddington limit, leading to a saturated accretion rate typically lower than the classical Bondi prediction by orders of magnitude. At sufficiently low densities, the enthalpy flux of the spherical inflow contributes substantially to the emergent luminosity, in which case the inner disk luminosity is reduced and the accretion flow enters an unsaturated state. A criterion is derived to estimate whether the accretion flow is saturated. We further demonstrate the applicability of our model by considering fallback accretion in failed supernova systems. The predicted nearly constant emergent luminosity, constrained by the generalized Eddington limit, provides a possible explanation for the observed luminosity plateau in the failed supernova candidate M31-2014-DS1. The presence of an inner disk also provides sufficient radiation to avoid the under-luminous predictions of a purely spherical fallback model extending down to the central object. Our model provides a framework for studying radiation-regulated accretion flows in systems where low but finite angular momentum leads to the formation of an accretion disk within a larger-scale quasi-spherical inflow.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 4

## 98. [An end-to-end experimental pipeline for automated track identification for paleo-detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17008) <a id="link98"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17008

**Authors:** Claudio Galelli, Alessandro Testi, Lorenzo Caccianiga

**Abstract:** Paleo-detectors aim to exploit the damage tracks left in natural minerals by cosmic messengers over geological times to reconstruct past fluxes of energetic particles. For cosmic rays, the expected signal is abundant, and the tracks are micron-scale, so they can be revealed by etching and read out with conventional optical microscopy; what remains challenging is turning a bulk sample into a calibrated track-length spectrum that can be quantitatively confronted with theoretical predictions. In this work we present the experimental pipeline we developed for this purpose within the INFN PRImuS experiment, from sample preparation and automated multi-focus microscopy to track recognition with OptimusPrimus, a two-stage machine-learning algorithm, and to a forward-folding model that propagates geometric sectioning, etching and the measured detection response into simulated spectra. The pipeline was built and validated on chemically etched obsidian where the recovered track-length distribution is statistically compatible with expert annotation. In parallel, we developed and characterized a dry argon-plasma etching protocol for halite, an evaporite mineral of particular interest as a low-background cosmic-ray paleo-detector. Since every stage of the pipeline is mineral-agnostic, with only the trained networks and the detection response to be re-derived from annotated data, we are now applying it to plasma-etched halite with the aim of identifying cosmic-ray-induced tracks.
