## 99. [First Experimental Results of Space Object Detection Using the InteRFeX Array](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13537) <a id="link99"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.13537

**Authors:** Bhaskar Ahuja, Thomas Crowther, Steven Tingay, Andrew Guidi, Luca Gentile, Marco Martorella

**Abstract:** This paper presents the first experimental validation of InteRFeX, a non-uniform receive only (passive) array located at the Nova Systems Space Precinct in Peterborough, Australia, designed for space domain awareness applications. It operates over a frequency range of 70 to 300 MHz and is capable of functioning in a range of observational modes, including as a coherent passive bistatic radar for space surveillance, which is the focus of this work. InteRFeX exploits commercial broadcast transmissions as illuminators of opportunity, combining cross-ambiguity function processing for bistatic range and range-rate retrieval with MUSIC-based direction-of-arrival (DoA) estimation for angular localization. The International Space Station (ISS) serves as the experimental target, with measurements evaluated against Two-Line Element (TLE) orbit predictions. Taking advantage of the dual-polarized array, all four reference--surveillance polarization combinations are evaluated, revealing differences in detection rate and mean signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) over the observation pass, with bistatic-range RMSEs of approximately 1 km relative to the TLE-derived reference. Independently, the MUSIC-based DoA estimates exhibit a mean angular separation of approximately $0.45^\circ$ from the TLE-derived reference directions. The angular deviations are further decomposed into along-track and cross-track components, enabling a more detailed characterization of the DoA offsets relative to the TLE-derived reference trajectory. The processing framework also includes a first-order bistatic link-budget analysis to estimate the expected SNR over the observation geometry. These results demonstrate the feasibility of passive-radar space domain awareness using radio-interferometric array technology and establish the proposed signal-processing pipeline as a basis for a dedicated coherent passive-radar mode within InteRFeX.

## 100. [What Does 2D Coronal Seismology Measure?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17433) <a id="link100"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17433

**Authors:** Zihao Yang, Yuhong Fan, Giuliana de Toma, Sarah Gibson, Matthias Rempel

**Abstract:** Two-dimensional (2D) coronal seismology based on coronagraphic imaging spectroscopy has emerged as a promising method for mapping the coronal magnetic field, but the physical meaning of its inferred quantities under optically thin line-of-sight (LOS) integration remains uncertain. We investigate this problem by synthesizing coronagraphic observables from a global wave-driven magnetohydrodynamic model with a long LOS extent and a structured corona, and by applying the same analysis procedures used for observations. We compare the diagnosed density, wave propagation direction, phase speed, and magnetic-field strength with different LOS-weighted reference quantities from the model. We find that the measured density and the wave propagation direction are both well represented by LOS emissivity-weighted quantities. In contrast, the phase speed is not controlled by emissivity weighting alone: the LOS distribution of wave velocity amplitude also plays an important role. As a result, the inferred magnetic field is better represented by combining the emissivity-weighted density with an amplitude-dependent effective speed. Emissivity weighting nevertheless remains a reasonable approximation, and a useful baseline, over much of the field of view, especially where the LOS does not cross strongly structured plasma-sheet-like regions. These results clarify the physical interpretation of 2D coronal seismology and provide guidance for future coronal magnetic-field tomography and model-constrained applications.

## 101. [Decomposing the Kinematics of SNR G180.0-01.7 with Medium-Resolution Spectra](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12880) <a id="link101"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.12880

**Authors:** Juan-Juan Ren, Wei Zhang, Chao-Jian Wu, Chao Liu, Jian-Jun Chen, Hong Wu

**Abstract:** The exceptionally large angular diameter and intrinsic optical faintness of the Galactic supernova remnant (SNR) G180.0-01.7 (S147) have historically hindered a comprehensive spectroscopic census of its filamentary structure. We present the first dedicated medium-resolution (R ~ 7500) spectroscopic study of S147 using the LAMOST Medium-Resolution Spectroscopic survey of Galactic Nebulae (MRS-N). By implementing multi-Gaussian decomposition on the H$α$, [NII], and [SII] emission complexes and applying combined spectroscopic and spatial criteria, we disentangle the shock-ionized filaments of the SNR from the pervasive foreground and background Diffuse Ionized Gas (DIG). Our analysis reveals a distinct bimodal radial velocity distribution for the SNR: a low-velocity population tracing the decelerated forward shock at a dense cavity wall, and a high-velocity population (with absolute radial velocities of ~ 70 $\pm$ 30 km/s) corresponding to ejecta within a low-density cavity. The co-existence of these populations provides kinematic evidence for the "supernova-in-a-cavity" scenario. Furthermore, we detect enhanced [SII]/H$α$ ratios in the DIG along the eastern and northern boundaries of the remnant, signaling shock compression of the ambient interstellar medium and highlighting remnant's multifaceted feedback.

## 102. [Simulation and Deep Learning Inversion for Full-sky Energetic Neutral Atom Imaging at the Heliospheric Boundary](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17414) <a id="link102"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17414

**Authors:** Weining Wang, Rong Lin, Jiansen He, Hong Zou, Yongfu Wang, Jiali Chen, Yuguang Ye, Weihong Shi, Wei Zhang, Xi Lu, Die Duan, Tianhang Chen, Linghua Wang, Qiugang Zong

**Abstract:** The distribution of energetic neutral atom (ENA) differential flux density at the heliospheric boundary is the key to revealing the interaction mechanism between the solar wind and the interstellar medium. The Helios ENA Rapid Mapper (HERMA) is a pre-research project for a new ENA detection concept and technical principle, which is designed to provide technical support for refined detection with its advantages of wide energy range coverage and high spatial resolution. To verify the high spatiotemporal resolution rapid imaging capability of the HERMA, this study restored the particle response characteristics of the HERMA (spatial modulation grid + 256-pixel linear array silicon detector) based on Geant4 simulation, and we compared the performance of the traditional inversion algorithm with the convolutional neural network (CNN) inversion algorithm using INCA data as the benchmark. The results show that under different sampling times, the source distribution inverted by the CNN algorithm has a higher degree of agreement with the ideally expected distribution, and the CNN algorithm exhibits better stability and accuracy than the traditional algorithm under the conditions of low signal-to-noise ratio and short staring time. This study provides core technical support for the engineering verification and on-orbit application of the HERMA instrument, fills the technical gap in wide-energy-range and high-resolution ENA detection, and helps to deepen the research on the interaction mechanism between the solar wind and the interstellar medium at the heliospheric boundary.

## 103. [ELISA: A Bayesian X/$γ$-ray Spectral Analysis Toolkit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14587) <a id="link103"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.14587

**Authors:** Wang-Chen Xue, Sheng-Lun Xie, Chao Zheng, Shao-Lin Xiong, Xiao-Bo Li, Yong Chen, Peng Zhang, Yan-Qiu Zhang, Jia-Cong Liu, Chen-Wei Wang, Wen-Jun Tan, Yue Wang, Jin-Peng Zhang, Zheng-Hang Yu

**Abstract:** Forward-folded X/$γ$-ray spectral fitting increasingly benefits from Bayesian workflows to better capture counting statistics, background uncertainties, and complex parameter degeneracies, while fully reproducible end-to-end implementations are still not always straightforward to deploy in routine analyses. We present ELISA (Efficient Library for Spectral Analysis), an open-source Python toolkit that streamlines the full spectral analysis loop for X/$γ$-ray astronomy: data loading, model construction, inference, and model validation and comparison. ELISA couples a JAX-accelerated forward model with a unified fitting interface, supporting both rapid optimization and modern Bayesian sampling. Built-in diagnostics make convergence checks and predictive assessment a standard part of the workflow, motivating principled model refinement when needed. Using a spectral fitting example of the Insight-HXMT Crab observation, we find substantial improvements in sampling efficiency compared with XSPEC's default MCMC implementation. ELISA is available at https://github.com/wcxve/elisa and on PyPI at https://pypi.org/project/astro-elisa.

## 104. [Ultralight scalars and invisible gravitons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14119) <a id="link104"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.14119

**Authors:** Massimo Giovannini

**Abstract:** Unless the tensor to scalar ratio is small enough, the non-adiabatic mode associated with the ultralight bosons may be dangerously comparable with the adiabatic spectrum of curvature inhomogeneities. We point out here that this statement depends on the timeline of the post-inflationary expansion rate. Provided the decelerated evolution is slower than radiation for a sufficiently long period, the relative weight of the non-adiabatic and adiabatic components does not conflict with the direct constraints coming from the large-scale observations. Conversely the presence of ultralight bosons excludes the presence of long stages expanding faster than radiation. The synthesis of the light nuclei and the spectrum of the relic gravitons at high frequencies set the relevant constraints on the duration of the decelerated stages, on the delayed dominance of radiation and on the mass range of the ultralight scalars.

## 105. [From the QBO to Rieger-type periods: Explaining extreme solar events by tidally triggered magneto-Rossby waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16428) <a id="link105"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16428

**Authors:** F. Stefani, G. M. Horstmann, G. Mamatsashvili, T. Weier

**Abstract:** It has recently been demonstrated that the occurrence of extreme solar events is governed by a phase-stable period close to 1.723 years (629.29 days). According to our previously developed tidally-synchronized solar dynamo model, this is one of the periods resulting from two-planet spring tides exerted by the tidally dominant planets Venus, Earth and Jupiter, which trigger magneto-Rossby waves in the solar tachocline. Overall, the squared sum of these three waves incorporates nine periods ranging from 59 to 629 days. Using a standard Rayleigh test, we demonstrate that all nine of these periods can be identified in the data on extreme solar events. For the latter, we use a combination of 72 ground-level enhancement (GLE) events with various X-flare data, the number of which increases when the threshold is successively lowered from X10 to X1. To identify the spectral peaks, it is necessary to analyze the sidebands that emerge due to the modulation with the longer-term periods such as the 11-year Schwabe cycle and the third harmonics of the Hale cycle. In this framework, the seminal Rieger period of 154 days is re-interpreted as the lower sideband of 146 days which is the quarter of the synodic period of Venus and Earth.

## 106. [JWST Imaging of Edge-on Protoplanetary Disks. V. Flux Ratio Reversal and Disk-edge Structure in the Flying Saucer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17136) <a id="link106"></a>

**Abstract:** We present near- and mid-infrared broadband imaging observations of the edge-on protoplanetary disk 2MASS J16281370-2431391, a.k.a. the 'Flying Saucer', obtained with NIRCam and MIRI on board JWST. These data are combined with archival optical and near-IR broadband imaging from HST, millimeter dust continuum emission from ALMA, and optical lightcurves from the K2 and TESS for a comprehensive multi-wavelength study. The JWST observations reveal a reversal of the flux ratio between the two reflection nebulae at near-infrared wavelengths (2-3 $μm$), which can be explained by a misaligned inner disk. For the first time, JWST reveals the bright lining along the disk edge at 2 and 3 $μm$, arising from forward scattering of background photons by dust grains near the $τ\approx 1$ layer. By combining the dust lane thickness measured with HST and JWST, we find the dust grains up to at least $\sim$3$μ$m are vertically mixed throughout the disk and have undergone significant growth. In addition, comparison of earlier HST/NICMOS J and K band images with those from JWST/NIRCam reveals variability in the scattered-light morphology, as well as measurable proper motion of the system with respect to the background stars, confirming that the Flying Saucer is co-moving with the young star population of $ρ$ Ophiuchi.

## 107. [Discovery of 51 pulsars in a reanalysis of the Green Bank North Celestial Cap Survey and a revised pulsar census](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17280) <a id="link107"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17280

**Authors:** R. Sengar, L. Vleeschower, D. L. Kaplan, G. Y. Agazie, M. Bailes, A. Cassity, H. T. Cromartie, M. E. Decesar, T. Dolch, W. Fiore, E. Fonseca, R. S. Lynch, E. Parent, S. M. Ransom, I. H. Stairs, J. van Leeuwen

**Abstract:** We present results from a reanalysis of the Green Bank North Celestial Cap (GBNCC) survey, conducted at 350 MHz with the Green Bank Telescope between 2009 and 2020, covering the sky with declinations $δ>-40^\circ$. We searched the survey data using an FFT-based periodicity search, with candidate selection extending to very low spectral signal-to-noise ratios and summing up to 32 harmonics. From the 60 promising candidates identified in this search, we confirmed 51 new pulsars using the Green bank and CHIME. We also used a direct folding search method (DFSM) to search for slow pulsars between 500 ms and 10 s. Of the 51 pulsars identified in the FFT search, 43 fall within this period range, all of which were found by the DFSM. The DFSM also found an additional pulsar that had been missed during the inspection of the FFT candidates. Most of the new discoveries are canonical pulsars, with spin periods ranging from 299 ms to 4.4 s and dispersion measures from 10 to 192 $\rm pc \, cm^{-3}$. We also found a binary millisecond pulsar with a spin period of 3.2 ms in a 4.5-d orbit. We redetected 855 pulsars in the data, 825 of which were found in the FFT search. Within the effective parameter space of our periodicity search, the FFT search recovered $\sim$98 % of the known pulsars within the survey sensitivity limits. After accounting for previously reported pulsars whose observations were not processed or whose parameters lay outside our search range, the updated GBNCC census now includes 964 pulsars.

## 108. [Beyond the Kullback--Leibler divergence: the likelihood-ratio distribution as a detectability measure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17186) <a id="link108"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17186

**Authors:** Amirhossein Samandar, Mikel Martin Barandiaran, Linn Htat Lu, Andrew H. Jaffe, Deyan P. Mihaylov, Glenn D. Starkman, Cynthia Trendafilova, Yashar Akrami, Stefano Anselmi, Javier Carrón Duque, Craig J. Copi, Fernando Cornet-Gomez, Arthur Kosowsky, Anna Negro, Ricardo G. Rodrigues, Andrius Tamosiunas

**Abstract:** The Kullback--Leibler (KL) divergence is the expectation value of the log-likelihood ratio between two specified models, evaluated with respect to one of the two models. It provides an average measure of the distinguishability of the models. We examine its connection to discovery potential and the accuracy of parameter estimates when one model represents a null hypothesis, and the alternative is a family of models characterized by one or more continuous parameters. For data drawn from a multivariate Gaussian distribution, we derive the log-likelihood-ratio distribution and its cumulants under two such hypotheses. We then search over the alternative model's parameter space and use peak prominence, the height of a likelihood-profile peak above its surrounding baseline, as an operational detection statistic. Repeating this search on simulated data drawn from the null model allows us to calibrate the prominence threshold for a specified false-positive rate. We study a toy Gaussian model as well as three cosmological examples involving cosmic topology, the tensor-to-scalar ratio, and dark energy. In all these cases, detectability of a particular model depends not only on KL divergence, but also on the size of the model parameter space, the likelihood-ratio distributions, and the distance of the model from any parameter space boundaries. The relationship between KL divergence, discovery potential, and parameter estimation must therefore be calibrated for each family of models and detection statistic under consideration.

## 109. [MRI Turbulence versus $α$-Viscosity in Circumbinary Disks: Implications for Gravitational-Wave Dephasing and Accretion Variability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17212) <a id="link109"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17212

**Authors:** Mudit Garg, Yan-Fei Jiang

**Abstract:** Our understanding of gas torques on massive black hole binaries (MBHBs) relies on hydrodynamical (HD) simulations of circumbinary disks (CBDs) in which an $α$-viscosity models angular momentum transport. However, CBDs around MBHBs near merger are hot, magnetized plasmas where transport is driven by magnetohydrodynamical (MHD) turbulence sustained by the magnetorotational instability (MRI). We test how well an $α$-viscosity captures MRI turbulence with 3D global Athena++ simulations of a locally isothermal CBD around an equal-mass, circular MBHB, run in HD and MHD from the same initial torus on identical grids, so that only the transport mechanism differs. Excising the region within twice the binary separation, $r<2~a_{\rm bin}$, allows $1000$ orbits of evolution, several times longer than previous MHD simulations. Both disks deliver the same accretion rate, and the gas beyond $2~a_{\rm bin}$ exerts a positive torque in both, yet the MHD torque is $3.5$ times larger and $3$ times more variable. Near the midplane, transport is similar in the disk body but three times weaker in MHD at the cavity edge, where $2$-$3$ times more mass piles up in a more eccentric cavity, raising the torque. The accretion rate is modulated mainly at twice the binary frequency in HD, but by a persistent lump at the cavity edge and its beat with the binary in MHD. The outer-disk torque therefore implies a contribution to the gas-induced phase shift in the gravitational-wave signal of LISA binaries $3.5$ times larger than in $α$-disk models, and a different variability signature for LSST and Roman.

## 110. [A Pipeline for the AI-Assisted Analysis of Imaging X-ray Polarimetry Explorer Observations of Black Hole X-ray Binaries and Limits on Their Polarization Variability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14185) <a id="link110"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.14185

**Authors:** B. Domine, B. Liu, H. Krawczynski, M. Kundu, Shravan Vengalil Menon, Fang Zhou

**Abstract:** The Imaging X-ray Polarimetry Explorer (IXPE) has opened a new window into the high-energy Universe, with the capability to measure the arrival time, arrival direction, energy, and, for the first time with the required sensitivity, the polarization of X-rays from black holes, neutron stars, and other cosmic sources of X-rays. In this paper, we discuss the use of Artificial Intelligence (AI) agents for the analysis of IXPE data. The pipeline combines an immutable code base with evolving knowledge databases. The generation of analysis projects makes use of a literature data base to judge the novelty of the proposed analysis projects. We emphasize that the analysis requires two types of expert knowledge: detailed instructions about how to reduce the X-ray data, and expert knowledge about how to interpret the results. We employ several levels of auditors that scrutinize the analysis plan, the analysis scripts, and the analysis results for data and method adequacy. The pipeline includes a staggered approach to interpret the analysis results. We present exemplary results from the pipeline applied to IXPE observations of Black Hole X-ray Binaries (BHXRBs) including comparisons of results from the analysis pipeline with published results and a systematic search for polarization degree and polarization angle variability in long IXPE observations. We critically discuss the performance of the pipeline and future avenues for improving it.

## 111. [An Ultraviolet Instrument on the Habitable Worlds Observatory: Considerations and Capability Trades on the Multi-Object, Integral-field, and High-resolution modes of a potential UVI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12974) <a id="link111"></a>

**Authors:** Brian Fleming, Kevin France, Dmitry Vorobiev, Jeanette Donber, Eric Frater, Thomas Delker, Vicki Herde

**Abstract:** The Habitable Worlds Observatory (HWO) is NASA's next-generation astrophysics platform, delivering transformative capability in the search for life and study of the origins of the universe. Amongst the instruments under consideration for HWO is a multi-purpose ultraviolet spectrograph and imager that provides a deep toolbox for some of the most pressing science investigations of the next several decades. In this paper, we present a basic draft model of a potential UVI, including a multi-object spectrograph, far ultraviolet imager, high resolution spectrograph, and integral-field spectrograph. The draft designs for these channels of the UV instrument serve as a basis for a general discussion of the instrument architecture and design challenges, while also identifying potential trades, enhancements, and other considerations that the HWO project will need to address as it moves into the formulation phase. We conclude with a presentation of several scientific simulations that have helped guide the capabilities of the draft design. This paper is intended to serve as a summary of UV instrument modes, technology, and potential design ahead of planned instrument studies in the late 2020's, and then Phase A in the early 2030's.

## 112. [TESS flares and chromospheric variability in the Pleiades, the Hyades, and Praesepe](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16772) <a id="link112"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16772

**Authors:** A. Görgei, K. Vida, B. Seli, K. Oláh, P. Sági, B. Schmercz, R. M. Roettenbacher, Zs. Kővári

**Abstract:** Magnetic activity is a result of the interaction between rotation and dynamo processes in cool stars with outer convection zones. Since open cluster members share a common age, distance, and chemical composition, they provide an opportunity to study the magnetic activity of cool stars in a controlled astrophysical context. We used 120s cadence observations from the Transiting Exoplanet Survey Satellite (TESS) to study the flare activity of stars in three open clusters with different ages: the Pleiades (100 Myr), the Hyades (700 Myr) and Praesepe (700 Myr). We used the Ohio State Multi-Object Spectrograph (OSMOS) at MDM Observatory with the goal of identifying coronal mass ejections (CMEs) in the Pleiades and Praesepe. We investigated the flaring characteristics of the cluster members using the TESS light curves: we identified the flares, then divided the dataset into different categories based on the effective temperature and light curve amplitude. We calculated the flare energies and investigated the flare frequency distributions. We studied the dynamic spectra of the H$α$ line and its environment to search for indication of CMEs. The power-law indices of the flare frequency distributions obtained in our analysis span the 1.8-2.8 range. We found the highest flare rates in the Pleiades for all investigated spectral types. Our results show that the flare rate of M dwarfs is similar in the Pleiades and the Hyades while for K-type stars the flare rate in the Pleiades is an order of magnitude higher. Our analysis reveals that the flare rate in M stars changes with their effective temperature: the rate is highest for T$_{eff}$ $\sim$3200-3300 K -coinciding with the transition between partially and fully convective stars - with smooth decrease in flare rate towards cooler and hotter M dwarfs. We used the MDM/OSMOS spectra to place an upper limit on the CME rate.

## 113. [Grand minima and intermittency in near-critical convection-driven dynamos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16314) <a id="link113"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16314

**Authors:** P. J. Käpylä

**Abstract:** Context: Solar magnetism exhibits long-term variations on timescales of decades to centuries. Aims: The main aim is to study how the supercriticality of the dynamo affects the variability of magnetic fields on timescales relevant to the Sun. Methods: Three-dimensional rotating magnetohydrodynamic simulations in a spherical wedge geometry are used to produce self-consistent convection-driven dynamos. The supercriticality of the dynamo is controlled by varying the magnetic diffusivity. Results: The main result is that long-term variability of the dynamo increases with decreasing supercriticality of the dynamo and that cases nearest to the onset of dynamo action show extended grand minima-like periods of low activity. The simulations fall in three classes where the least supercritical cases show the largest variations of magnetic activity and where the flow including differential rotation is almost unaffected by the magnetic field. In the cases with the highest supercriticality the magnetic field is permanently strong and differential rotation permanently quenched, both having relatively little temporal variation. In an intermediate regime both the magnetic fields and differential rotation exhibit strong anticorrelated fluctuations. Conclusions: The occurrence of grand minima in the Sun has been conjectured to be due to a weakly supercritical dynamo. The current results support this idea and suggest that the absence of grand minima in earlier global dynamo simulations is because their large-scale dynamos were too supercritical. Furthermore, the current results suggest that the effect of the magnetic field on differential rotation is negligible if the average rms-field is less than about 2~kG.

## 114. [Mapping the Contracting Corona in GS 1354-64 with Insight-HXMT and XRISM: Evolving X-ray Lags and Quenched Reflection across a Complete State Transition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15569) <a id="link114"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.15569

**Authors:** Kaushik Chatterjee, Min Wei, Jie Liao, Biswaraj Palit, Santanu Mondal, Honghui Liu, Chandra B. Singh, Sandeep K. Rout, Ashutosh Tripathi, Mayukh Pahari, Guobao Zhang, Zejun Jiang, Brajesh Kumar, Wei Wang, Hsiang-Kuang Chang, Xiaowei Liu

**Abstract:** We present a comprehensive broadband spectro-temporal analysis of the dynamically confirmed Galactic black hole transient GS 1354-64 during its 2025-26 outburst, using near-simultaneous observations from Insight-HXMT and XRISM. The high spectral resolution of the XRISM-Resolve microcalorimeter, coupled with the high-cadence broadband coverage of Insight-HXMT, reveals a dramatic evolution in the inner accretion geometry. During the early hard-intermediate state, the spectrum was dominated by a hot Comptonizing corona accompanied by relativistically broadened reflection features. As the outburst progressed, the corona cooled efficiently, the photon index steepened (Gamma >~ 2.5), and the reflection signatures, including the broad Fe K-alpha emission, were quenched, yielding a featureless, disk-dominated continuum. Concurrently, Type-C quasi-periodic oscillations (QPOs) evolved from 0.25 Hz to 6.52 Hz. Cross-spectral timing analysis revealed that the broadband X-ray time lags changed from pronounced positive (hard) delays (~160 ms) at low frequencies to near zero as the source softened. However, a statistical analysis incorporating the measurement uncertainties shows that the broadband lags lack a significant monotonic correlation with the QPO frequency, photon index, or fractional rms. Instead, the scattered lag behavior suggests a competition between positive Comptonization delays and negative thermal reverberation. While the spectral softening and QPO acceleration provide strong evidence for a geometric contraction of the corona (whether interpreted via Lense-Thirring precession or the Two-Component Advective Flow (TCAF) paradigm), the broadband timing properties underscore the turbulent and inhomogeneous nature of the inner flow as the advancing Keplerian disk drives the source into the soft state.

## 116. [TractorJAX: GPU-Accelerated SPHEREx Forced Photometry and the Trade-offs of Regularization and External Priors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16007) <a id="link116"></a>

**Authors:** Hyeonguk Bahk, Ho Seong Hwang

**Abstract:** We present TractorJAX, an open-source GPU reimplementation of the catalog-forced mode of The Tractor. We use it to test whether regularization or external flux priors enable reliable fitting of reference catalogs that extend below the depth directly constrained by undersampled SPHEREx images. For full-depth regularized fits, TractorJAX fits $100\times100$-pixel cutouts at 11.9 ms each on a single NVIDIA H100 GPU. In simulated images of the Abell 2537 galaxy-cluster field, extending the fitted catalog from $m_z<21$ to the full DESI Legacy Imaging Surveys DR10 catalog increases the scatter in fractional flux residuals by 34%, even with regularization. We also test the methods using SPHEREx observations in nine fields, assessing the number of reliably measured sources and photometric-redshift accuracy. Requiring S/N $>5$ in more than half of the 102 channels, we find that the regularized full-depth fit yields 40% fewer sources than the truncated fit. For sources meeting this requirement in both fits, the normalized median absolute deviation of the photometric redshifts, $σ_{\rm NMAD}$, is 17% larger in the full-depth fit. External flux priors recover nearly as many sources as the $m_z<21$ fit, but increase $σ_{\rm NMAD}$ by 0.0015 for the common calibrated sample. Neither regularization nor the external flux priors tested here simultaneously preserve the sample size and photometric-redshift accuracy achieved by the truncated fit. Our results suggest that the optimal fitted-catalog depth cannot be determined from image-level performance alone, but should be selected through end-to-end tests tailored to the intended science measurement.

## 117. [Estimating the spectral resolution of a radio data cube from its noise properties](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17223) <a id="link117"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17223

**Authors:** P. Serra, G. Mulas, K. M. Hess, P. Kamphuis, T. Westmeier, Y. Ascasibar, J. M. van der Hulst

**Abstract:** We present a new method to estimate the spectral resolution of a radio data cube from its noise properties. We assume that the data cube is the result of the convolution of a spectrally-uncorrelated cube with a constant convolution kernel K. We demonstrate that K and the resolution, defined as K's integral, can be estimated thanks to the analytical relation between the autocorrelation of the noise spectra extracted from the cube and the power spectrum of the kernel. We discuss a number of algorithms that make this method robust in the realistic case of a finite number of spectra and channels, real signal and artefacts, and we present the results obtained for a number of datasets from the literature. We further highlight that this method is applicable to any data cube that meets our assumptions independently of the observing frequency or the exact nature of the cube's "third" axis, e.g., it could be used to measure the time resolution of transient-search data cubes. We make this method available as part of the open-source software tool spectre.

We investigate the form of the luminosity-richness relation, finding the relation using the full 57 cluster sample is statistically consistent with using only the 45 matched clusters, and will require the deeper data of future eRASS releases (or dedicated X-ray follow-up) to explore further potential differences. Using external data from XMM, ACT and SPT, we find detections of 5 of the 12 unmatched clusters, indicating the presence of virialised gas. Casanova, U. Cotti, J. Cotzomi, S. Coutiño de León, E. De la Fuente, N. Di Lalla, R. Diaz Hernandez, M. A. DuVernois, J. C. Díaz-Vélez, C. Espinoza, N. Fraija, S. Fraija, A. Galván-Gámez, J. A. Iriarte, S. Kaufmann, D. Kieda, K. Leavitt, W. H. Lee, H. León Vargas, A. L. Longinotti, G. Luis-Raya, K. Malone, O. Martinez, J. Martínez-Castro, J. A. Matthews, P. Miranda-Romagnoli, P. E. Mirón-Enriquez, J. A. Morales-Soto, E. Moreno, M. Mostafá, M. Najafi, L. Nellen, M. U. Nisa, R. Noriega-Papaqui, M. Osorio-Archila, E. Ponce, Y. Pérez Araujo, E. G. Pérez-Pérez, C. D. Rho, D. Rosa-González, M. Roth, A. Sandoval, J. Serna-Franco, M. Shin, A. J. Smith, Y. Son, R. W. Springer, O. Tibolla, I. Torres, R. Torres-Escobedo, L. Villaseñor, X. Wang, Z. Wang, I. J. Watson, H. Wu, S. Yu, S. Yun-Cárcamo, X. Zhang, H. Zhou, C. de León

**Abstract:** Previous observations of the DA 495 region, which contains the pulsar wind nebula (PWN) DA 495 and other very-high-energy (VHE; $>100~\textrm{GeV}$) gamma-ray emitters, showed inconsistent results across different VHE experiments, including differences in the measured flux of DA 495 and in the number of reported sources. Our new dedicated analysis using 3071 days of HAWC data resolves these discrepancies. We identify three extended sources, HAWC J1952+2924 (DA 495), HAWC J1954+2837, and HAWC J1958+2848, all with curved spectra, and find a hint of a fourth source. For DA 495, a multi-wavelength analysis, including our results, radio, X-ray, and other gamma-ray data, reveals that its extended emission is best described by leptonic interactions with an inferred magnetic field of $B\sim10~μ\textrm{G}$ and that hadronic components are not required. On the other hand, we interpret the other two sources, which have no confirmed counterparts, as TeV halos associated with nearby pulsars, PSR J1954+2836 and PSR J1958+2846, respectively. These results reinforce the interpretation of DA 495 as a leptonic-dominated PWN and add two candidates to the growing population of TeV halos.

## 120. [Blackbody evolution and circumstellar medium properties of broad-lined H-rich superluminous supernovae](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16527) <a id="link120"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16527

**Authors:** Tuomas Kangas, Takashi Nagao, Uliana Pylypenko, Steve Schulze, Lin Yan

**Abstract:** Broad-lined H-rich superluminous supernovae (SLSNe II) tend to show signs of interaction, including a UV excess compared to a blackbody function. However, a quantitative analysis of the CSM configuration and the UV excess has so far not been performed for most of the literature sample. Additionally, SN 2023gpw showed signs of asymmetry in its peculiar blackbody and spectral evolution, raising the question of whether similar properties can be found in other SLSNe II. We aim to characterize the circumstellar environment of SLSNe II by searching for signs of asymmetry, quantifying the UV excess, and investigating any IR echoes produced by dust. Diversity among SLSNe II is also explored. We fit blackbody functions to the SEDs of SLSNe II to quantify the UV excess luminosity and its evolution. We use this to investigate mass-loss rates required to produce the excess through interaction. We also investigate the line profiles in SLSN II at late times and compare line velocities to the blackbody radius evolution. We examine public WISE IR images and place constraints on IR echoes. We find mass loss rates of $\sim10^{-4}$ to $\sim0.03\, \mathrm{M_\odot}$~yr$^{-1}$ when assuming all interaction luminosity emerges in the UV, i.e., conservative lower limits for the total mass loss rate. Radii calculated from line velocities in late-time spectra are mostly too large for a spherical interaction photosphere, only compatible with the blackbody radius evolution if the CSM is non-spherical with a low filling factor. This is in some cases supported by a mismatch between spectroscopic and blackbody expansion velocities. Considerable diversity is seen in the sample, probably as a result of different viewing angles and CSM masses. WISE images seem too shallow to observe IR echoes in SLSNe II beyond $z\sim0.1$, but in the nearest event, SN 2020jhm, a likely IR echo is detected, suggesting pre-existing dust.

## 121. [In-flight Timing Calibration of the Follow-up X-Ray Telescope on Board the Einstein Probe](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12943) <a id="link121"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.12943

**Authors:** Juan Zhang, Xiaofan Zhao, Dawei Han, Hao Wang, Yong Chen, Mingyu Ge, Shijie Zheng, Shumei Jia, Haisheng Zhao, Weiwei Cui, Hanlong Peng, Wentao Ye, Yaoming Duan, Youli Tuo, Zhen Ji, Fuli Ma, Chengkui Li, Ju Guan, Jingjing Xu, Jin Wang, Liming Song

**Abstract:** After the launch of Einstein Probe (EP), pulsars are observed to calibrate the timing system of the onboard Follow-up X-ray Telescope (FXT) by measuring the times of arrival (TOAs). FXT has two independently operating modules, namely FXTA and FXTB, each equipped with the Wolter-I optics and the pnCCD camera. For scientific observations, each pnCCD has three distinct readout modes that differ in frame cycles, the full frame mode (FF, 50 ms per frame), the partial windowed mode (PW, 2.2 ms per frame), and the timing mode (TM, 23.68 $μ$s per row, 9.4352 ms per frame). Thus, 1RXS J170849.0--400910 with the spin period of 11 s is observed in FF, PSR B1509--58 (150 ms) in PW, and Crab pulsar (33 ms) in TM as well as PSR B1821--24 (3.05 ms) and PSR B1937+21 (1.56 ms). The TOAs of these pulsars measured by FXTA and FXTB are compared with the results of NICER, and for Crab pulsar with the Jodrell Bank Monthly Ephemeris (JBE). Results verify that the timing system of FXTA and FXTB in FF, PW, and TM modes are consistent with each other, and are in good alignment with NICER. The measured advance of the Crab pulsar's main peak relative to JBE is $357^{\pm14(stat)}_{\pm54(sys)} μ$s in FXTA and $394^{\pm14(stat)}_{\pm67(sys)} μ$s in FXTB. Based on measurements of PSR B1821--24 and PSR B1937+21, the preliminary absolute timing accuracy is estimated to be 6 $μ$s for FXTA and 31 $μ$s for FXTB in TM mode. Additionally, the systematic delay of FXTA timing results relative to FXTB is found to be $20^{\pm19(stat)}_{\pm21(sys)} μ$s.

## 122. [ASKAP Monitoring of the Rubin Deep Drilling Fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13011) <a id="link122"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.13011

**Authors:** Dougal Dobie, James Freeburn, Iris de Ruiter, Katie Auchettl, James Coulson, Brent Miszalski, Anais Möller, Nikhil Sarin

**Abstract:** In the past decade, time domain astronomy surveys across the multi-messenger spectrum have developed "alert streams" which circulate observational information in real-time to the wider astronomical community. Despite this being standard practice for optical, gamma-ray and gravitational wave time domain astronomy for several years now, this kind of infrastructure has not yet been deployed at-scale for radio telescopes. We are conducting a long-term monitoring program that targets the Rubin Deep Drilling Fields using the Australian SKA Pathfinder (ASKAP). The primary goal of this program is to provide serendipitous radio observations of all objects of interest in these fields, and circulate that information in as close to real-time as possible. In this manuscript we outline the survey plans and status, the infrastructure we have developed to disseminate this information and an initial data release comprising 5 months of ASKAP observations of each field (although only 3 months overlaps with Rubin) conducted on an approximately 10 day cadence with a noise level of $\sim 60\,μ$Jy. As part of this data release, we present updated high-cadence radio light curves to the tidal disruption event AT2018hyz and the supernova SN2024abfo along with the discovery of two new radio variables, a possible tidal disruption event ASKAP J095711.5+051205 and an in-progress extreme scattering event ASKAP J005026.4-422958. The scientific results from this program along with the infrastructure developed to acquire them will lay the foundation for the integration of future radio imaging surveys into the broader multi-messenger alert stream, leading the way for major facilities such as the Schmidt Observatory System and the SKA.

## 123. [Milani and Juventas Optical Navigation Images in Support of the Hera Radio Science Experiment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14791) <a id="link123"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.14791

**Authors:** Edoardo Gramigna, Ozgur Karatekin, Birgit Ritter, Iosto Fodde, Fabio Ferrari, Felice Piccolo, Paolo Tortora, Riccardo Lasagni Manghi, Marco Zannoni, Ryan S. Park

**Abstract:** The European Space Agency's Hera mission aims to advance planetary defense by investigating the Didymos binary asteroid system following NASA's DART impact on Dimorphos. Incorporating simulated CubeSat images into Hera's orbit determination framework significantly improves positional accuracy, reducing uncertainties by up to three orders of magnitude for Milani and one for Juventas. This data enable more precise estimation of key parameters such as asteroid masses, rotational states, and moments of inertia, with up to 90% uncertainty reduction in Dimorphos' dynamical state under chaotic scenarios. Sensitivity analysis reveals the influence of pointing accuracy, image noise, and imaging frequency. Additionally, dedicated scientific investigations using Dimorphos-focused CubeSat images further advance understanding of the system. These findings underscore the critical role of CubeSat images in refining navigation and advancing science, offering valuable insights for future small-body missions.

## 124. [Assessing the impact of background subtraction on a dust-free corona](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15895) <a id="link124"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.15895

**Authors:** Erika Palmerio, Cooper Downs, Phillip Hess, Benjamin J. Lynch, Ryder Davidson, Ronald M. Caplan, Jon A. Linker

**Abstract:** Context. White-light observations of the solar corona contain contributions from both the electron-scattered K-corona and the dust-scattered F-corona. Because the diffuse F-corona dominates the measured intensity at altitudes of approximately 4 $R_{\odot}$ and above, background subtraction techniques are routinely employed to isolate the electron corona in coronagraph observations. Aims. Since no direct observational "ground truth" for the K-corona exists, the amount of coronal signal that is inadvertently removed by background subtraction methods remains unknown. We aim to quantify, for the first time, the impact of background subtraction on the inferred large-scale structure and brightness of the solar corona. Methods. We use a 32-day simulation based on a novel near-real-time, data-assimilative, time-evolving magnetohydrodynamic model to generate synthetic white-light observables. Because these synthetic observations are free from dust and instrumental stray-light contributions, they provide direct access to the "true" K-corona and enable a quantitative assessment of the effects of background subtraction. Results. We find that background subtraction generally preserves the large-scale morphology of the electron corona, including the locations and shapes of major coronal structures. However, the procedure systematically modifies the relative brightness of different features and removes a non-negligible fraction of the K-coronal signal. Conclusions. Although standard background subtraction techniques successfully recover the overall appearance of the electron corona, they can alter its quantitative photometric properties. These findings should be considered when interpreting coronal brightness measurements and deriving physical properties from processed white-light observations.

**Novelty:** 4

## 125. [Integration of a CME Flux Rope Model with Magnetic Field Draping in Heliospheric Simulations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16004) <a id="link125"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16004

**Authors:** Ranadeep Sarkar, Jens Pomoell, Emilia Kilpua, Eleanna Asvestari

**Abstract:** Accurate prediction of the magnetic structure of interplanetary coronal mass ejections (ICMEs) remains a key challenge in space weather forecasting. Despite several efforts using global MHD modeling, it remains difficult to deduce a flux-rope solution that can accurately and reliably reproduce the magnetic structure of a CME. We present FROST (Flux Rope embedded in the Solar Magnetic Field), a new flux-rope model implementation within the European Heliospheric Forecast Information Asset (EUHFORIA), featuring an initially force-free toroidal flux rope embedded in the low-coronal magnetic field. FROST employs a novel embedding technique that self-consistently generates a draping magnetic field around the flux-rope, ensuring continuity of the normal magnetic field component at the CME boundary. The dynamics of the flux-rope in the low and middle corona is solved by a non-uniform advection constrained by the observed kinematics of the event. This results in a global, non-toroidal, stretched loop-like magnetic structure. At heliospheric distances, the evolution is modeled as an MHD process using EUHFORIA. We validate the FROST model implementation by simulating the CME event of 2013 April 11 and comparing the results with in situ magnetic field measurements at 1 au. The modeled magnetic field profiles of the flux-rope exhibit strong agreement with the observations, with correlation coefficients ranging from 0.64-0.93 for Bx, 0.92-0.95 for By, and 0.82-0.87 for Bz component of the magnetic field. These results demonstrate the capability of the proposed flux-rope model to significantly improve ICME magnetic field prediction, highlighting its strong potential for advancing space-weather forecasting capabilities.

**Novelty:** 4

## 126. [The XMM Cluster Survey: Cross-Calibration of eROSITA and XMM using Dark Energy Survey Y3 Galaxy Clusters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16741) <a id="link126"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16741

**Authors:** J. E. Pilling, D. J. Turner, P. A. Giles, A. K. Romer, R. Wilkinson, P. J. Rooney, M. Hilton, S. Bhargava, J. Hall, R. Wang, A. Abdulghafour, B. J. Maughan

**Abstract:** We present the cross-calibration of the eROSITA and XMM telescopes, using galaxy clusters detected by redMaPPer from the first three years of Dark Energy Survey observations (DESY3RM). We use the X-ray: Generate and Analyse (XGA) Python module to measure eROSITA properties, and validate our methodology using a deep pointed eROSITA CAL-PV observation of A3158, and a sample of 119 clusters from the expanded HIgh FLux Galaxy Cluster Sample (eeHiFLUGCS). Next, we construct a sample of DESY3RM clusters matched to XMM observations analysed by the XMM Cluster Survey (XCS), and to clusters in the first release of the eROSITA All-Sky Survey (eRASS1). This produced a sample of 114 DESY3RM clusters with both XMM and eROSITA detections. Our resulting X-ray temperatures determined from eROSITA were compared to eRASS1 catalogue values, showing consistency between the two analyses. Comparisons between XMM and eROSITA temperatures reveal a systematic offset, with XMM temperatures 28$\pm$7% higher on average. Finally, we fit $L_{\rm X,soft}$-$λ_{\rm RM}$ and $T_{\rm X}$-$λ_{\rm RM}$ scaling relations for both eROSITA and XMM using the same cluster sample, finding minimal differences in the $L_{\rm X,soft}$-$λ_{\rm RM}$ relation but a clear normalisation offset in the $T_{\rm X}$-$λ_{\rm RM}$ relation, particularly for the highest richness systems. This study will enable future work combining eROSITA and XMM observatories for DESY3RM clusters.

## 127. [Formation Dynamics of a Rotating Solar Spicule-like Event and Associated Energy-Mass Transport](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13173) <a id="link127"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.13173

**Authors:** Liufan Gong, Lei Ni, Guanchong Cheng, Jiajia Liu, Quan Xie, Robert Erdélyi

**Abstract:** We performed three-dimensional radiative magnetohydrodynamic(3D RMHD) simulations in an upward-directed (positive-polarity) open magnetic field with an initial background strength of 10 G. We identified vortex-associated spicule-like events and carried out a detailed analysis of one representative event. Prior to the formation of this spicule-like event, a photospheric vortex appears and twists the magnetic field lines, then develops upward into higher atmospheric layers, forming a magnetic tornado system. This event is generated during this process. At the periphery of this magnetic tornado, twisted magnetic field lines with negative vertical components reconnect with both external positive-polarity background fields and central positive-polarity fields. The magnetic reconnection sites rise and rotate together with the developed magnetic tornado, and many jet-like substructures in this event are closely associated with plasma upward ejections from these reconnection regions. A vertical force analysis shows that, for this event, plasma uplift in the photosphere and lower chromosphere is dominated by the pressure-gradient force, whereas in the upper chromosphere, the Lorentz force gradually becomes dominant. At a height of about 4 Mm above the solar surface, this event produces a mean upward mass flux density on the order of $10^{-7}~{\rm kg~m^{-2}~s^{-1}}$ and a mean upward Poynting flux density of approximately $525~{\rm W~m^{-2}}$. The wave-mode decomposition results show that the energy transport associated with the rotational spicule-like event is dominated by the Alfvén mode. Alfvén waves, fast-mode waves, and slow-mode waves carry energy into the lower corona in an approximate ratio of $10:7:3$.

## 128. [The nearby Type II supernova 2024ggi beyond 600 days: circumstellar interaction and dust formation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17140) <a id="link128"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17140

**Authors:** L. Ferrari, G. Folatelli, C. P. Gutiérrez, A. Méndez, C. Ashall, K. Medler, K. Ertini, H. Kuncarayakti, N. Morrell, M. Stritzinger, W. Hoogendam, E. Hueichapán, R. Cartier, J. P. Anderson, M. Bersten, L. Galbany, J. L. Prieto, C. Cárdenas Uribe, S. Vazhayil

**Abstract:** The Type II SN 2024ggi is one of the nearest core-collapse SNe to have exploded in recent decades. Its proximity has enabled extensive multiwavelength monitoring from early times through the nebular phase. Signatures of late-time interaction between the SN ejecta and hydrogen-rich circumstellar material (CSM) have recently been reported. Here we extend its study using late-time optical photometry and spectroscopy from 570 to 820 d after explosion. We investigate the emergence and evolution of late-time ejecta-CSM interaction and constrain the geometry of the interaction region and the properties of newly formed dust. Comparisons with other interacting SNe II and with synthetic spectra are presented. We model the H$α$ profile with the Monte Carlo radiative-transfer code Damocles, including both gas and dust components. Spectroscopic signatures of interaction precede the photometric flattening. The spectra evolve from ejecta-dominated to interaction-dominated as H$α$ develops a broad, boxy profile. The optical light curves subsequently flatten relative to the $^{56}$Co decay rate at approximately 650-670 d. The emission lines show a systematic blue-to-red asymmetry that requires a dust component newly formed within the ejecta. The inferred dust mass formed in the ejecta is $10^{-6}-10^{-5}$ M$_\odot$ if the grains are amorphous carbon or a carbon-silicate mixture, but can reach up to $10^{-3}$ M$_\odot$ for pure silicates.

## 129. [The Thousand-Pulsar-Array programme on MeerKAT - XX. Co-existing interacting periodicities and polarization modulation in PSR J1901-0906](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16571) <a id="link129"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16571

**Authors:** Jui-An Hsu, Patrick Weltevrede, Geoff Wright, Michael J Keith, Aris Karastergiou, Xiaoxi Song

**Abstract:** We present a comprehensive analysis of the complex subpulse modulation in PSR J1901-0906 using data from the Thousand-Pulsar-Array (TPA) programme. Our analysis reveals the coexistence of three distinct periodicities in the subpulse modulation of both profile components, which obey a beat relation. We also detect additional spectral beat features caused by the modulation of these co-existing periodicities by investigating the Fourier phase correlation in two-dimensional fluctuation spectra. Moreover, the single-pulse polarization states are shown to be modulated and synchronized with the three co-existing periodicities observed in total intensity. Such synchronization has been reported for only a small number of pulsars, but this discovery marks the only one where this synchronization involves multiple co-existing periodicities. The modulation beats are similar to, yet more complex than, the beat system previously identified in PSR J1514-4834 with higher-order harmonics playing a significant role. The coexistence of the observed beat-like periodicities in PSR J1901-0906 implies that they are not independent, but rather originate from an underlying, interconnected physical system within the pulsar magnetosphere. Building upon the same geometric framework previously applied to PSR J1514-4834, we demonstrate that a feedback system with interacting rotating carousels successfully reproduces the complex subpulse modulations observed in PSR J1901-0906 and naturally explains the beat relations.

## 130. [Hera Orbit Determination Covariance Analysis of Didymos Binary Asteroid with the Full Two-Body Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14784) <a id="link130"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.14784

**Authors:** Edoardo Gramigna, Riccardo Lasagni Manghi, Marco Zannoni, Paolo Tortora, Michalis Gaitanas, Ioannis Gkolias, Kleomenis Tsiganis, Ryan S. Park, Alex Meyer, Petr Pravec

**Abstract:** Standard planetary radio science analyses may be inadequate for accurately interpreting data from the Hera mission at the Didymos system due to the anticipated challenge posed by the coupling between the translational and rotational motions of the asteroids. A more precise dynamical model, the full two-body problem model, which includes a mutual gravitational potential, may be required to fit the Hera data properly. This work presents a covariance analysis of the Hera radio science experiment using this advanced model. The tested dynamical scenarios are based on recent lightcurve observations indicating that Dimorphos is in an excited non-principal axis spin state that is, on average, tidally locked. Three non-principal axis scenarios are analyzed using a multi-arc local estimation approach instead of the global one adopted in previous investigations involving non-chaotic states. The results show that tumbling or chaotic cases significantly improve the recovery of the bodies' moments of inertia due to the enhanced observability of the system's dynamics from the excited spin state. However, the local estimation approach slightly degrades the accuracy of the mass estimation by a factor of 1.3-3 and Dimorphos' relative orbit by about one order of magnitude. Developing a dedicated constrained multi-arc filter is thus required to improve the estimation accuracy of the Didymos system's dynamical state further. Nevertheless, these results still meet the Hera radio science requirements.

## 131. [Graph Learning with Multiplicative Aggregation for Solar Filament Fragment Association Modeling in Full-disk H$α$ Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17291) <a id="link131"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17291

**Authors:** GaoFei Zhu, GangHua Lin, Xiao Yang, Suo Liu, Yu Liu, ZhiSheng Gao, Cheng Zeng

**Abstract:** Solar filaments are prominent features in H$α$ observations. During their evolution, they often exhibit complex morphological and topological changes, which, due to line-of-sight projection effects, appear as fragmentation and merging. These phenomena make it difficult to reliably track solar filaments using automated methods, as the same structure may appear different over time. To address this issue, we formulate filament fragment association as a graph-based learning problem, where individual fragments are represented as nodes and candidate pairwise relationships are modeled as edges to infer whether fragments belong to the same filament. Furthermore, we propose a novel graph neural network (GNN) that incorporates multiplicative aggregation layers to enhance feature aggregation in the graph, replacing conventional additive aggregation with a multiplicative mechanism to better capture information between fragments. Experimental results demonstrate that the proposed method achieves reliable performance in filament fragment association, validating the effectiveness of the graph-based formulation and the proposed multiplicative aggregation mechanism.

## 133. [Decay-Assisted Leptogenesis during Preheating](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17084) <a id="link133"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17084

**Authors:** Wenbin Zhao

**Abstract:** We revisit the non-thermal leptogenesis mechanism during preheating using the quantum Boltzmann equations. This framework allows us to study the production and decay of heavy right-handed neutrinos in a consistent manner. Due to their fermionic nature, right-handed neutrino production can saturate after a few inflaton oscillations when their occupation numbers approach unity. Right-handed neutrino decays can deplete their occupation numbers, allowing more particles to be produced and generating a larger lepton asymmetry. Compared with the case where right-handed neutrinos decay only after production has finished, we find that the final baryon asymmetry can be enhanced by two orders of magnitude.

## 134. [Rotating Vector Model predictions in millisecond pulsar magnetospheres with dipole and quadrupole field components](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15779) <a id="link134"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.15779

**Authors:** Anna V. Bilous

**Abstract:** The rotating vector model (RVM) is widely used to interpret pulsar radio polarization under the assumption of a dipolar magnetic field. Motivated by tentative evidence for non-dipolar magnetic structure in millisecond pulsars provided by recent modeling of surface thermal X-ray pulsations, we examine how a global quadrupole component modifies position-angle (PA) behavior within the classical RVM framework. We numerically integrate magnetic field lines in static vacuum magnetospheres composed of a centered dipole component that is inclined with respect to the spin axis, and a centered quadrupole component aligned with it. We then compute the open field line regions (OFLRs), emission skymaps, and PA curves for a range of dipole inclinations, relative quadrupole strengths, and light cylinder radii corresponding to two trial spin periods of 1\,ms and 5\,ms. We find that quadrupole-modified magnetospheres produce a wide variety of PA behaviors and substantially alter the morphology and total area of OFLRs, with the OFLR area varying non-monotonically with quadrupole strength and, in some cases, exceeding that of the corresponding pure dipole. Depending sensitively on the field configuration and viewing geometry, three broad PA regimes can be distinguished: a) nearly dipolar PA swings that are largely insensitive to emission altitude; b) moderately distorted, altitude-dependent PA tracks that can bias dipolar RVM fits; and c) strongly dispersed PA behavior associated with emission from different OFLRs, which can remain incompatible with a single dipolar PA track even within narrow emission altitude ranges. These results demonstrate that dipole-like PA curves do not uniquely imply a purely dipolar magnetic field, while global quadrupole components alone are insufficient to reproduce the full complexity of observed millisecond pulsar PA profiles.

**Novelty:** 4

## 135. [TESS Pulsation Timing Reveals the JWST Calibration Star HD 158485 as a Double-pulsator Binary](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14371) <a id="link135"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.14371

**Authors:** Chenglong Lv, Ali Esamdin, Shuguo Ma, Shahidin Yaqup, Haozhi Wang, Ayzada Jmahali, Jianwei Yao, Kedieryemu Kamili

**Abstract:** HD~158485 is an A-type $δ$~Scuti star in the CALSPEC network and the JWST absolute-flux calibration program. Gaia DR3 assigns it a binary classification probability close to unity but provides no orbital solution. Using TESS observations from Sectors 14--26, 40--41, 47--60, and 73--86, we measure the pulsation phases of six $δ$~Scuti modes. The frequencies form two groups with opposite light-travel-time delays, revealing two pulsating components. A direct light-curve forward model yields an orbital period of $P_{\rm orb}=382.80^{+0.80}_{-0.76}$~d, eccentricity $e=0.242^{+0.045}_{-0.046}$, projected light-time amplitudes of $195.8^{+9.2}_{-8.9}$ and $239.7^{+6.8}_{-6.8}$~s, and a dynamical mass ratio of $q=M_B/M_A=0.818^{+0.046}_{-0.045}$. A blind prediction based only on the first two long TESS campaigns reproduces the third campaign without updating the orbital parameters, with a Pearson correlation coefficient of $r=0.983$. Its held-out rms is 83.8~s, compared with 289.9~s when the period is fixed at 365.25~d. A 5.15-$σ$ Hipparcos--Gaia EDR3 proper-motion anomaly independently indicates unresolved astrometric motion. The unresolved companion can also affect spectrophotometric predictions: a conditional PB2-constrained two-atmosphere model differs from the corresponding single-star atmosphere prediction by 1.6\% over 1--5~$μ$m and 2.5\% over 5--28~$μ$m. HD~158485 therefore shows that a calibration-relevant binary can lack a catalogued orbital solution while two independent pulsation clocks reveal and measure its orbit.

## 136. [The Long-Term Radial Velocity Stability of the COS G130M/1291 Mode](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15819) <a id="link136"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.15819

**Authors:** John Debes, David M. French, Leonardo A. Dos Santos, Travis Fischer, Svea Hernandez, Ravi Sankrit

**Abstract:** We investigate the long-term radial velocity (RV) stability of one of the Far-UV (FUV) modes on the Cosmic Origins Spectrograph (COS). We do so by using a standard white dwarf, WD~0308-565, that has been observed over a large fraction of COS's operational lifetime of 17 years. We demonstrate that for modest signal-to-noise COS can return an RV stability of $<$3 km s$^{-1}$ over a baseline of 15 years. For higher SNR and a smaller number of epochs, we obtain $σ_{\mathrm V}=600$ m s$^{-1}$. We also show that the absolute radial velocity accuracy for this target is consistent with results from ground-based optical spectroscopy. We discuss some observational strategies and astrophysical objects that may be well suited to pushing to the very RV limit of the instrument. Utilizing COS for precision RV work opens a new scientific avenue for space-based UV astronomy as COS approaches 20 years of operations and hints at what may be possible with the stability of a high-resolution UV spectrograph on future missions such as the Habitable Worlds Observatory.

## 137. [Occultation polarimetry as a probe of unresolved polarization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15811) <a id="link137"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.15811

**Authors:** A. G. Tevzadze

**Abstract:** Context: Low polarization in an unresolved source may reflect weak local polarization or spatial cancellation between strongly polarized regions. An occultation can break this spatial degeneracy by differentially sampling the source as the occulting boundary moves. Aims: We develop a general theoretical framework for occultation polarimetry and determine what information can be inferred directly from unresolved full Stokes light curves, without reconstructing a polarization image or prescribing a source model. Methods: For a stationary, spatially incoherent source undergoing monotonic occultation, we relate Stokes variations to the polarization sampled by an arbitrarily curved occulting boundary and derive an exact variance decomposition of the normalized Stokes vector. Results: The polarization deficit decomposes exactly into three non-negative contributions: local polarization deficit, mixing within limb samples, and mixing between their mean polarization states. The last is directly measurable and provides a sharp, model-independent lower bound on total spatial mixing. The associated covariance tensor further characterizes the direction and correlations of the resolved polarization variations, including those between linear and circular polarization. Finite sampling preserves the bound, which can be strengthened through complementary occultation trajectories. Conclusions: Occultation polarimetry provides a powerful observational technique for extracting quantitative, model independent constraints on spatial polarization mixing directly from unresolved Stokes light curves. It offers a means to reveal both the extent and the form of polarization structure otherwise inaccessible at the available angular resolution.

**Novelty:** 4

## 138. [Bayesian exploration of weakly interacting models of dark matter admixed neutron stars based on multi-density constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15713) <a id="link138"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.15713

**Authors:** Payaswinee Arvikar, Prashant Thakur, Sarmistha Banik

**Abstract:** We present a unified Bayesian analysis of Higgs-portal, neutron-decay, and axion-like-particle (ALP)-mediated dark matter in neutron stars within the single-fluid approximation. Using a common relativistic mean-field description of hadronic matter, we combine finite-nucleus and heavy-ion collision data, chiral effective field theory, NICER measurements of four pulsars, and GW170817. The individual dark-sector parameters remain weakly determined, while model-dependent shifts in nuclear skewness reveal the sensitivity of the inferred hadronic equation of state to the assumed dark-matter mechanism. The three scenarios yield substantially overlapping mass-radius and tidal-deformability predictions, with median maximum masses near $2.1\,M_\odot$. For $1.4\,M_\odot$ stars, the Higgs-portal model predicts higher $f$-mode frequencies and shorter damping times than the neutron-decay and ALP models, although their credible regions overlap. Comparison with three consistently reanalysed two-fluid models gives the largest Bayesian evidence to neutron decay: under the adopted priors, its Bayes factors relative to the Higgs-portal and two-fluid alternatives are approximately $24$-$37$, but only $2.2$ relative to ALP. This ranking concerns complete dark-matter models and does not establish a general preference for either fluid description or evidence for a dark component itself. Improved nuclear constraints and future gravitational-wave asteroseismology could provide complementary information to disentangle the hadronic and dark-sector contributions.

## 139. [Universal relations for hybrid stars and their quark cores: a systematic scan of the quark-matter parameter space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17230) <a id="link139"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17230

**Authors:** J. P. Carlomagno, G. A. Contrera, A. G. Grunfeld, G. Lugones

**Abstract:** Universal, equation-of-state (EOS) insensitive relations among the global properties of compact stars are well established for hadronic neutron stars and for self-bound strange stars. We ask whether analogous relations hold for the quark core of a hybrid star, and whether the core can be constrained from the surface. Describing quark matter with the three-flavor MIT bag model with repulsive vector interactions, matched to the hadronic DD2 EOS through a Maxwell construction, we scan the full $(B^{1/4},G_V)$ plane and obtain 1112 hybrid EOSs with maximum masses above $2\,M_\odot$, broadly compatible with current multimessenger constraints. For every stable configuration we evaluate the mass, radius, moment of inertia, and tidal deformability both at the stellar surface and at the boundary of the quark core. Global quantities follow the relations of hadronic stars---the $I$--Love relation to within $0.5\%$---while core quantities follow those of self-bound strange stars with median deviations of $6$--$9\%$. We explain the latter by expanding the structure of the core in powers of its compactness: to first order, the core mass, baryon mass, moment of inertia, and tidal deformability depend only on the compactness and on the properties of the hadron--quark interface, chiefly the ratio $ξ=p_{\rm tr}/\varepsilon_+$ of transition pressure to quark energy density. The strange-star relations are the $ξ\to0$ member of this family, and the deviations from them measure $ξ$. The fractional core mass and radius of all configurations follow the normalized mass profile of the star, and no global observable allows the core size to be inferred. Evanescent cores dominate the viable parameter space, while sizable cores require low bag constants and strong vector couplings.

## 140. [OGLE-2016-BLG-0523: A Low-mass Brown Dwarf or super-Jupiter in a $>100$ au Orbit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13942) <a id="link140"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.13942

**Authors:** Hongjing Yang, Weicheng Zang, Yoon-Hyun Ryu, Andrzej Udalski, Yuchen Tang, Michael D. Albrow, Sun-Ju Chung, Andrew Gould, Cheongho Han, Kyu-Ha Hwang, Youn Kil Jung, In-Gu Shin, Yossi Shvartzvald, Jennifer C. Yee, Sang-Mok Cha, Dong-Jin Kim, Hyoun-Woo Kim, Seung-Lee Kim, Chung-Uk Lee, Dong-Joo Lee, Yongseok Lee, Byeong-Gon Park, Richard W. Pogge, Przemek Mróz, Michał K. Szymański, Jan Skowron, Radoslaw Poleski, Igor Soszyński, Paweł Pietrukowicz, Szymon Kozłowski, Krzysztof A. Rybicki, Patryk Iwanek, Krzysztof Ulaczyk, Marcin Wrona, Mariusz Gromadzki, Mateusz J. Mróz, Jiyuan Zhang, Shude Mao, Hongyu Li, Qiyue Qian

**Abstract:** To search for very wide-orbit binary-lens events, we cross-match microlensing events detected by OGLE-IV and KMTNet between 2011 and 2024. After removing contaminants from variable stars, we identify OGLE-2016-BLG-0523 as a microlensing event showing two short-lived bumps in its light curve, with the two separated by 8.36 yr. We investigate three scenarios to explain the two bumps, including a binary-lens single-source (2L1S), a single-lens binary-source (1L2S), and two unrelated single-lens single-source events (2$\times$1L1S). The 1L2S interpretation is excluded due to its poorer fit to the data, while the 2$\times$1L1S scenario is disfavored by the low event rate, unphysical total source flux, and unphysical color for one of the two putative sources. For the surviving 2L1S interpretation, the Einstein timescales of the two bumps are $8.343 \pm 0.076$ days and $3.231 \pm 0.047$ days, respectively, implying a binary mass ratio of $q = 0.15$. A Bayesian analysis indicates that the primary lens is most likely a low-mass M dwarf, while the companion is probably a low-mass brown dwarf or super-Jupiter. The inferred projected separation is about 450 au, making the companion the widest low-mass object discovered via microlensing to date. Our discovery highlights both the promise and challenges of identifying very wide, low-mass companions through microlensing. Although upcoming facilities such as Roman and Earth 2.0 space telescopes will improve sensitivity to such events, overcoming degeneracies and distinguishing them from variable phenomena will remain essential for building a robust statistical sample.

## 141. [A first view of interstellar Fe-bearing dust grains with XRISM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14172) <a id="link141"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.14172

**Authors:** I. Psaradaki, L. Corrales, E. Costantini, M. Mehdipour, D. Rogantini, H. Takahasi, R. Ballhausen, P. Chakraborty, T. Nakagawa, R. K. Smith, S. Zeegers

**Abstract:** Understanding the reservoirs of iron (Fe) in our Galaxy is crucial for probing the mechanisms that drive dust grain growth in the interstellar medium (ISM). High-resolution X-ray absorption spectroscopy offers a powerful tool to investigate the chemistry of dust grains, particularly those containing Fe, through spectroscopic features near photoabsorption edges known as X-ray Absorption Fine Structures (XAFS). With the Resolve spectrometer aboard XRISM, we can now examine the Fe K-edge (7.1 keV) owing to its improved spectral resolution in this energy range. This enabled us to probe the dust grain chemistry in high Galactic column densities. In this work, we present a pilot study on dust grain chemistry through the Fe K-edge of the XRISM Performance Verification target GX 340+0. This source is a persistently bright low-mass X-ray binary with a hydrogen column density of 6.6x10^22 cm^-2 along the line of sight, providing sufficient optical depth at the Fe K-edge. By fitting combined dust and gas models, we find that Fe is depleted by 95% into dust along the line of sight towards the source. Moreover, we find evidence that 38% of the solid-phase Fe is in metallic iron, 47% in iron sulfides, and the remainder in silicates. The large contribution from metallic iron and iron sulfides differs from what is typically inferred from X-ray studies of diffuse ISM and may suggest that that these compounds become more significant reservoirs of Fe in higher column density Galactic sightlines.

## 142. [Perpendicular transport in isotropic magnetic turbulence. The role of stochastic drifts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13865) <a id="link142"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.13865

**Authors:** F. Pucci, P. Blasi, O. Pezzi, C. Evoli, F. Malara

**Abstract:** In cosmic ray propagation through turbulent magnetic fields, transport perpendicular to the mean field plays an important role in various astrophysical environments. Numerical simulations have shown that parallel and perpendicular diffusion coefficients exhibit different energy scalings, causing their ratio to increase with energy, but a comprehensive theoretical explanation remains elusive. Here, we investigate perpendicular diffusion using test particle simulations in synthetic isotropic turbulence spanning 6-7 orders of magnitude in wavenumber, together with a theoretical extension of nonlinear guiding center theory that incorporates stochastic drifts. Our approach combines field line random walk and random guiding center drifts to describe perpendicular transport. We find that parallel and perpendicular diffusion coefficients exhibit the same energy scaling only for $r_L \lesssim 10^{-2} L_c$, where $r_L$ is the particle Larmor radius and $L_c$ is the turbulence coherence length. At higher energies, perpendicular diffusion develops a stronger energy dependence, consistently across different turbulence levels. Our theoretical model suggests that this behavior arises from stochastic drifts induced by turbulent magnetic field components parallel to the mean field.

## 143. [Reawakening of GS 1843+00: A Multiwavelength Study of its 2026 Giant X-ray Outburst](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14494) <a id="link143"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.14494

**Authors:** Gaurava K. Jaisawal, Georgios Vasilopoulos, Francesco Carotenuto, Hannah C. I. Wichern

**Abstract:** GS 1843+00 (Ginga 1843+009) is a poorly studied Be/X-ray binary system that underwent a major X-ray outburst in 2026 February-May after nearly sixteen years of quiescence. We present an X-ray, optical, and radio study of GS 1843+00 during this outburst, focusing on the evolution of the neutron star emission geometry, Be-disk connection, and possible outflow activity. We analyse three NuSTAR observations obtained at different stages of the outburst, complemented by archival RXTE and Chandra data, optical spectroscopy and imaging with the Nordic Optical Telescope, and a MeerKAT radio observation. NuSTAR observations reveal coherent X-ray pulsations around 29.7 s with a spin-up rate of $(-5.1\pm0.1)\times10^{-8}$ s s$^{-1}$ during the outburst. The pulse profiles show strong evolution with energy and outburst phase, with distinct morphologies even at comparable luminosities. The pulsed fraction varies between 1 and 16% and exhibits a complex reversal in energy dependence above 7 keV, increasing with energy during the first two observations and decreasing during the third. The 3-79 keV broadband spectra are described by thermal Comptonization with a blackbody and an Fe K$α$ emission line, with no evidence for a cyclotron absorption feature. Phase-resolved spectroscopy reveals variations in the Comptonization and blackbody parameters, indicating changes in the accretion-column structure. Optical spectroscopy confirms the presence of a Be-circumstellar disk through H$α$ and Paschen-series emission lines. No radio counterpart is detected, with a $3σ$ upper limit of 36 $μ$Jy beam$^{-1}$ at 1.28 GHz. Weaker H$α$ emission compared with previous epochs indicates that the Be-circumstellar disk was in a different state during the current episode, while the radio non-detection suggests that any associated outflow activity was faint or below the detection limit.

## 144. [Mid-IR Water-Vapor Seeing: Implications for ELT/METIS High-Contrast Imaging from VLTI Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14801) <a id="link144"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.14801

**Authors:** Abhirami S Raghu, Prashant Pathak, Olivier Absil, Benjamin Courtney-Barrer, Gilles Orban de Xivry, Julien Woillez, Alexis Matter, Philippe Bério, Roy van Boekel, Bernhard Brandl

**Abstract:** Direct imaging and characterization of rocky exoplanets remains one of the central challenges in exoplanetary science. The mid-infrared (mid-IR) offers a favorable high-contrast imaging (HCI) regime, as the planet-star contrast is more moderate than at shorter wavelengths. However, mid-IR observations with extremely large ground-based telescopes (ELTs) are strongly affected by spatio-temporal fluctuations in atmospheric water vapor (WV), which introduce chromatic wavefront errors that are largely invisible to adaptive optics systems operating at shorter wavelengths. This poses a significant challenge for mid-IR HCI instruments such as METIS, which aims to detect thermal emission from rocky exoplanets in the $N$-band (8--13 $μ$ m). To quantify WV-induced wavefront errors, we analyze $K$-band fringe-tracker data from the GRAVITY instrument at the VLTI and derive the optical path difference between group-delay and phase-delay measurements. This difference provides a direct measure of the differential WV column density ($Σ$) above the telescopes. We examine the dependence of the RMS differential WV column density ($σ_Σ$) on precipitable water vapor (PWV), airmass, seeing, and wind speed across multiple VLTI configurations. Among these parameters, PWV shows the strongest correlation with $σ_Σ$. We further explore the influence of the vertical PWV distribution on this relationship. Next, we use power-spectral-density analysis to quantify how increasing PWV modulates $Σ$ variability. The wavefront error associated with this phenomenon on ELT scale is approximately 15~nm RMS in the $L$-band and 175~nm RMS in the $N$-band, relative to the $K$-band. These results provide a quantitative framework for assessing and mitigating atmospheric WV effects in next-generation mid-IR HCI instruments.

**Novelty:** 4
