## 145. [The Population of Type~1 Active Galactic Nuclei in Dark Energy Spectroscopic Instrument. I. Profile-dependent Measurements of the Low-redshift Sample](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13218) <a id="link145"></a>

**Authors:** Zhu-Heng Yao, Wei-Jian Guo, Shuo Zhai, Yu-Chang Li, Cheng Cheng, Jun-Rong Liu, Shuang Jin, Antonios Katsianis, Jing Wang, Da-Wei Xu, Jian-Yan Wei

**Abstract:** We present a spectral analysis of a large sample of low-redshift type 1 active galactic nuclei (AGNs) selected from the first data release of the Dark Energy Spectroscopic Instrument (DESI DR1). For objects at $z\le0.9$, we perform spectral fitting and ML-assisted refinement, yielding 114,754 type 1 AGNs. We use two fitting schemes that differ only in the broad-line model: the broad Balmer components are fitted with either a Lorentzian function or a sum of Gaussians. The continuum and [O III] luminosity measurements agree closely between the two schemes, whereas the Gaussians scheme generally yields larger broad-H$β$ full width at half maximum (FWHM) values, larger virial black hole masses, and lower accretion rates. The adopted broad-Balmer profile also changes the decomposition of narrow Balmer emission. Specifically, the anti-correlation between [O III]$\lambda5007$/narrow H$β$ and accretion rate is stronger under the Gaussians scheme with a Spearman rank correlation coefficient $r_{\rm s}=-0.412$, than under the Lorentzian scheme with $r_{\rm s}=-0.206$; the same tendency is observed in the narrow-line Seyfert 1 galaxies (NLS1s) subset ($r_{\rm s}=-0.435$ and $-0.259$ for the Gaussians and Lorentzian schemes, respectively), suggesting that the Gaussians scheme may provide a more self-consistent separation of the broad and narrow H$β$ components. Moreover, the consistent correlations recovered under both profile prescriptions favor a continuous type 1 accretion-state sequence, with NLS1s occupying its high-accretion end rather than constituting a distinct population.

## 146. [Adiabatic evolution of hadronic neutron stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13926) <a id="link146"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.13926

**Authors:** Domenico Logoteta, Francesco Zeno Constanzo, Ignazio Bombaci, Sebastiano Bernuzzi, Albino Perego, David Radice

**Abstract:** In this paper we compute several structural properties of static and rotating neutron stars, considering both rigid and differential rotation. To this end, we use a finite-temperature beta-stable equation of state (EOS) extending the zero-temperature hadronic EOS derived by Fortin et al. (2017). We account for the formation of hyperons and the presence of a neutrino-trapped component. Our goal is to provide insights into the long-term evolution of a protoneutron star (PNS) formed after a core-collapse supernova (CCSN) or a remnant resulting from a binary neutron star merger (BNSM). The EOS is derived using a relativistic mean-field quantum many-body theory within the Hartree approximation. Coupling constants are determined using experimental data on hypernuclei and hypernuclear matter to better account for uncertainties in hyperon single-particle potentials. Our EOS models reproduce the typical features of cold neutron stars and PNSs for nucleonic and hyperonic compositions. In particular, they are consistent with measured masses (2.01 M_sun and 2.14 M_sun for PSR J0348+0432 and PSR J0740+6620) and dimensionless tidal deformabilities from GW170817. Thermal contributions act differently depending on composition and rotation state. Finally, our evolutionary scheme provides a simple way to interpret the long-term evolution of BNSM remnants or PNSs from CCSNe, complementing dynamical simulations unable to cover full evolutionary timescales.

## 147. [Beyond the Damped Random Walk: Multiband AGN Variability from SDSS to Rubin LSST](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14618) <a id="link147"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.14618

**Authors:** Izak Schmidlkofer, Zhirui Hu, Weixiang Yu, Hyungsuk Tak

**Abstract:** The flux variability of Active Galactic Nuclei (AGNs) is commonly modeled using the damped random walk (DRW), but increasingly rich multiband time-domain surveys motivate more flexible stochastic models that characterize variability beyond a single timescale and exploit dependence across bands. Multivariate continuous-time autoregressive moving average (MCARMA) processes extend the multivariate DRW to higher-order stochastic dynamics and provide a matrix-valued power spectral density for characterizing marginal variability and cross-band dependence. In a carefully selected Sloan Digital Sky Survey (SDSS) Stripe~82 sample, higher-order models are selected for 109 of 134 AGNs (81.3\%), with substantial rather than marginal support in most objects; convergence diagnostics indicate that this fraction is conservative. The fitted models also reveal strong cross-band dependence that generally decreases with wavelength separation. Motivated by this richer structure, we investigate its recoverability under Vera C. Rubin Observatory Legacy Survey of Space and Time (LSST) observing conditions. Model-order recovery depends strongly on the underlying stochastic behavior as well as source brightness and observing cadence. Recovery is high across many of the simulated settings, but deteriorates when the characteristic features of higher-order variability become difficult to distinguish from the multivariate DRW. Additional simulations show that this loss of distinguishability can persist over a range of underlying variability behaviors. These results show that stochastic structure beyond the multivariate DRW is prevalent in our selected SDSS sample, while its recoverability with LSST depends on both observational conditions and the form of the underlying stochastic variability. The methodology is implemented in the Python package mcarma.

## 148. [Revisiting the Multiwavelength Data of GRB 240825A: Implications for a Two-Stage Central Engine and a Dusty Environment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15703) <a id="link148"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.15703

**Authors:** Ren-Jie Xiong, Zi-Qi Wang, Xiao-Li Huang, En-Wei Liang

**Abstract:** Reanalyzing the multi-wavelength data of GRB 240825A, we identify two distinct prompt-emission episodes, i.e. an early, spectrally soft plateau and a subsequent bright, spectrally hard gamma-ray pulse. We show that this signature is consistent with a two-stage central engine model, in which the early soft plateau is powered by the dipolar spin-down radiation of a transient proto-magnetar, while the later hard pulse arises from a relativistic jet launched by fallback accretion onto the nascent black hole via the Blandford-Znajek mechanism. Adopting simulation-motivated initial black hole (BH) masses ($M_{\rm BH,0}$) and spins ($a_0$), our fits to the second episode yield total masses supplied to the post-collapse disk of $M_{\rm fb}=0.45$--$0.96 M_\odot$, fallback radii of $r_{\rm fb}=16.9$--$17.6 r_g$, and characteristic fallback timescales of $t_{\rm fb}=0.13$--$0.25$ s. The corresponding maximum inner accretion rates range from $0.18$ to $0.70 M_\odot \mathrm{s}^{-1}$, which is sensitive to $a_0$. Joint spectral fits with optical-X-ray afterglow data reveal a coupled temporal evolution of dust extinction and equivalent hydrogen column density. Incorporating this extinction evolution, an external forward-reverse shock model in a wind-like medium can account for the radio-to-GeV band afterglow data. Although ambient dust and the bright host galaxy may obscure any associated supernova, late-time optical reddening remains compatible with a supernova contribution. Applying the empirical relation between the prompt spectral peak energy and the SN peak bolometric magnitude gives an illustrative estimate of $M_{\rm bol,peak}=-19.46\pm0.35$ mag for a putative associated SN. These results support a two-stage evolution of the central engine and variable dusty environment of GRB 240825A, which provides insights into the nature of its progenitor.

## 149. [Anisotropic Radiation in Super-Eddington Active Galactic Nuclei: The Responsivity of Broad-Line Regions to Continuum Variability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15255) <a id="link149"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.15255

**Authors:** Feng-Na Fang, Pu Du, Jian-Min Wang

**Abstract:** Anisotropic ionizing radiation is a natural theoretical expectation for super-Eddington accretion in active galactic nuclei (AGNs), implying that the ionizing flux incident on the broad-line region (BLR) clouds differs from that measured along the observer's line of sight. Reverberation mapping studies have revealed systematically shortened time lags of the broad H$β$ emission line relative to continuum variations in super-Eddington AGNs, a phenomenon that can be naturally interpreted as a consequence of anisotropic ionizing continuum radiation. To further investigate this anisotropy, we perform a systematic analysis of the H$β$ line responsivity, defined as the ratio of the variability amplitude of H$β$ to that of the driving continuum, for both sub- and super-Eddington AGNs. By combining the effective responsivities measured relative to the driving continuum, after correcting for the continuum--emission-line delay, with photoionization calculations, we infer the ionizing radiation field received by their BLRs. We find that, in super-Eddington AGNs, the ionizing flux inferred from the H$β$ responsivity differs significantly from that observed along the line of sight, whereas the two are in closer agreement in sub-Eddington AGNs. These results provide compelling evidence for strong anisotropy of the incident ionizing radiation in super-Eddington AGNs. We conclude by discussing the broader implications of this anisotropy, including its potential relevance to the "little red dots" recently discovered by the James Webb Space Telescope.

## 150. [Internal Nucleon Properties and the Equation of State of Ultra-Dense Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17423) <a id="link150"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17423

**Authors:** Bikram Keshari Pradhan, Jérôme Margueron, Guy Chanfray, Hubert Hansen

**Abstract:** The question of the link between the nucleon properties and the equation of state of ultra-dense matter, both being composed of quarks and gluons, is investigated by adopting a soliton model to describe internal nucleon properties and a direct mapping between nucleon and quark matter to obtain the equation of state of ultra-dense matter. We compare $π+ω$ and $π+ω+ρ$ soliton models and analyze the presence of a pressure singularity at the nucleon center due to the $ρ$ field. This singularity impacts the equation of state in ultra-dense matter. Only the pressure derived from the canonical energy-momentum tensor has a singularity, while the Belinfante pseudo-gauge transformation of the energy-momentum tensor generates an internal pressure without a singularity. We then investigate predictions for the equation of state of ultra-dense matter based on different prescriptions for the nucleon pressure. It is shown that none of the prescriptions agree with the thermodynamical pressure defined in uniform matter. The mismatch between any equation of state and the thermodynamical one questions the simplicity of the direct mapping between finite-size nucleon properties and uniform matter as well as the role of the nucleon surface in the internal energy density and the pressure.

**Authors:** Jie Liu, Ligong Bian

**Abstract:** The first-order phase transition is central to electroweak baryogenesis and gravitational wave generation, yet thermal equilibrium effective potential analyses cannot describe the real-time non-equilibrium dynamics of the phase transition process. **Abstract:** Intermediate-orbit ($\sim 1$ AU) brown dwarfs with absolute mass measurements are essential for understanding the evolution of substellar objects. Although such systems remain rare, their number is now being enlarged thanks to astrometry. Here, we present the absolute mass measurements for HD 203771B, a massive brown dwarf orbiting a K dwarf ($M_\ast=0.79\pm0.08\ M_\odot$) with a period of 1140 days on an eccentric ($e\approx0.5$) and inclined ($i\approx 130^{\circ}$) orbit. We find that the peak of eccentricity distribution tends to gradually shift toward lower values from wide orbits ($a>20$ AU) to intermediate ($1<a<20$ AU) and inner ($0.1<a<1$ AU) regions. Such trend is plausibly due to disk friction during orbital evolution. Together with obliquity studies showing that brown dwarfs are mostly aligned, our result suggests that brown dwarfs may migrate inward through quiescent coplanar disk-driven processes.

## 153. [X-Shooter view of planetary nebulae microstructures I: The case of NGC 7009 knots](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13587) <a id="link153"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.13587

**Authors:** L. Konstantinou, S. Akras, K. Bouvis, P. Boumis, J. Garcia-Rojas, D. R. Gonçalves, K. N. Gourgouliatos

**Abstract:** The properties of NGC 7009 knots are investigated by employing UV-to-near-infrared data from the X-Shooter spectrograph. These structures belong to the category of low-ionization structures (LISs), which are characterized by enhanced emission from low-ionization species and whose origin remains enigmatic. Our main goal is to conduct the first dedicated spectroscopic study of LISs using X-Shooter, that covers a wide spectral range from the UV to the near-IR, simultaneously. As a pilot study, we focus on NGC 7009 to investigate its ionized, atomic, and molecular gas, aiming to better characterize the physical properties of its LISs and explore their largely unexplored molecular content. The spectrum was processed through the ESO Reflex pipeline, and the physical properties of LISs were derived with the latest version of PyNeb. More than 250 emission lines were detected in the spectra of NGC 7009 LISs, including rare, forbidden transitions from low- ionization species such as Fe+ and Ni+, as well as two transitions from P+, identified here for the first time in LISs. The detection of multiple shock tracers, together with the shock excitation of [Fe II] and [O I], as revealed by emission-line diagnostics, supports the presence of shocks at LISs. The richness of the spectrum allowed us to derive electron temperatures and densities at the LISs using both collisionally excited and optical recombination lines. In the near-IR spectrum of the LISs, nine ro-vibrational H2 emission lines were detected, seven of which were reported here for the first time. These lines were used to construct the H2 excitation diagram, providing new insights into the physical conditions of the nebular gas. The excitation of molecular hydrogen is most likely dominated by collisional processes through shocks.

**Novelty:** 4

## 154. [Modelling the Point Spread Function of ILMT images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14819) <a id="link154"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.14819

**Authors:** Naveen Dukiya, Bhavya Ailawadhi, Talat Akhunov, Monalisa Dubey, Sara Filali, Paul Hickson, Priyanshi Kumari, Gokul Singh Mehra, Kuntal Misra, Vibhore Negi, Kumar Pranshu, Jean Surdej, Sarvesh Kumar Yadav, Archana Gupta

**Abstract:** We have devised an approach to model the empirical Point Spread Function (PSF) of the images taken with the 4-meter International Liquid Mirror Telescope (ILMT). The characteristics of the ILMT PSF are influenced by the nature of the Liquid Mirror, as well as by instrument specific and atmospheric factors. As the telescope uses Time Delay Integration (TDI) mode for tracking the passing objects, the PSF varies along the declination axis due to differential motion and star-trail curvature. The 5-element optical corrector homogenizes the PSF to some degree, but does not completely eliminate the disturbing effects mentioned above. The PSF in the RA direction is changing due to the sky conditions. We show that we can successfully model the PSF of 4k$\times$4k chunks using PSFEx, accounting for second-order X-Y variability. Our software robustly reproduces the PSF and its variation across the field.

## 155. [Deep learning methods for gamma event selection in TAIGA-IACT image analysis in stereo-mode](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16269) <a id="link155"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16269

**Authors:** E. O. Gres, A. P. Kryukov, P. A. Volchigov, A. P. Demichev, J. J. Dubenskaya, D. P. Zhurov, S. P. Polyakov, E. B. Postnikov, A. J. Razumov

**Abstract:** In astrophysical complex TAIGA there are four Imaging Atmospheric Cherenkov telescopes (IACTs), located at a distance of 300-500 m from each other, due to which extensive air showers (EAS) generated by cosmic and gamma radiation are recorded by a single telescope or by multiple telescopes simultaneously (stereo-mode). The analysis of EAS images from two or more Cherenkov telescopes together is of particular interest, since for this approach the reconstruction of physical parameters of the primary gamma ray is much more accurate than in mono-observations. However, the gamma-ray event reconstruction strongly depends on the quality of the hadron background suppression of the classifier, therefore, the gamma event selection stage is one of the important ones in the further analysis of IACT data. We propose and study several neural network methods for gamma event selection in joint TAIGA-IACT image analysis. Neural network methods we suggested involves a single-stage analysis of IACT images from two telescopes, while the second method considers the formation of autoencoder-derived essential features that contain the EAS physical parameters, including the type of primary particle.

## 156. [Broadband 600- and 1200-GHz Subharmonically Pumped Schottky Diode Mixers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15657) <a id="link156"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.15657

**Authors:** Peter Sobis, Vladimir Drakinskiy, Karl Jacob, Axel Murk, Anders Emrich, Jan Stake

**Abstract:** A subharmonic mixer topology is introduced that uses quarter-wave stubs to create virtual RF/LO grounds and provide impedance matching in close proximity to the antiparallel Schottky barrier diode pair, thereby reducing the electrical length and enabling broadband operation. The RF/LO matching topology is demonstrated at 600 GHz and 1200 GHz, with good agreement between measured and simulated performance. The 600-GHz mixer demonstrates approximately 20% RF bandwidth with a DSB receiver noise temperature below 1300 K across most of the measured band, while the 1200-GHz implementation achieves DSB receiver noise temperatures below 3000 K over a broad frequency range. Both mixers are characterized at room temperature with approximately 2 mW of LO power. The 600-GHz mixer is used in the Submillimeter Wave Instrument (SWI) onboard ESA's Jupiter Icy Moons Explorer. Overall, these results demonstrate state-of-the-art room-temperature performance for broadband receiver front ends targeting atmospheric research and remote-sensing applications.

## 157. [SiO Maser Line-width Diagnostics of AGB Stars and Red Supergiants: The RSG-adjacent Region in the FWZI--Luminosity Plane](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16453) <a id="link157"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16453

**Authors:** Qiu-Yu Tao, Jun-ichi Nakashima, Yong Zhang, Zehao Zhang, Jia-Yong Xie, Chang Liu

**Abstract:** We investigate whether SiO maser line profiles can distinguish asymptotic giant branch (AGB) stars from red supergiants (RSGs) and identify unusual AGB-classified sources near the RSG regime. We compiled 2,956 detected SiO maser spectra for 1,551 objects and constructed reference samples of 1,068 O-rich AGB stars and 36 RSGs. The full width at zero intensity (FWZI) was reliably measured for 1,020 AGB stars and 32 RSGs. AGB stars are concentrated at narrow line widths: 81.5% have FWZI <10 km s^-1, whereas 81.2% of RSGs have FWZI >15 km s^-1 and none has FWZI <10 km s^-1. Infrared two-color diagrams provide only partial separation. Combining FWZI with luminosities based primarily on quality-filtered Gaia/Bailer-Jones distances, with independent literature distances for several RSGs, reveals a weak tendency for wider SiO maser lines at higher luminosities but no clean boundary. We therefore define an empirical RSG-adjacent region in the FWZI-luminosity plane, 15 <= FWZI <= 26.6 km s^-1 and 3.9 <= log(L/L_sun) <= 4.9, to characterize the high-luminosity, broad-line tail of the AGB-classified sample. Ten sources fall within this region. Using infrared SEDs, DUSTY fits, OH/H2O maser information, and variability, we find that six are most naturally interpreted as luminous AGB-like objects, two warrant follow-up to assess possible high-initial-mass AGB, RSG-like, or SAGB-related interpretations, and two are less securely constrained because of poor DUSTY fits. The RSG-adjacent region is therefore a physically mixed screening space, not a definitive classification box, for selecting high-priority follow-up targets.

## 158. [Physical characteristics of the Galaxy defining the oscillation period of librating orbits near the OLR of the bar](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17255) <a id="link158"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17255

**Authors:** A. M. Melnik, E. N. Podzolkova

**Abstract:** We studied librating orbits of stars near the Outer Lindblad Resonance (OLR) of the bar using the angle-action variables of Hamiltonian dynamics. The librating orbits change the direction of their elongation relative to the major axis of the bar in a limited range of angles. To calculate orbits, we used the model of the Galaxy with an analytical bar which reproduces well the distributions of the observed stellar velocities along the Galactocentric distance R derived from the Gaia DR3 data. We compared the values of the orbital oscillation period of a typical star obtained from the theory and with the use of numerical simulation. Both values were found to be in good agreement and are equal to 2.0 Gyr. It was shown that the change in the average orbital radius, <R>, of a star, calculated over the time interval of one radial oscillation, is regulated by the change in the direction of orbital elongation. As the orbit is tilted relative to the minor axis of the bar in the sense of Galactic rotation, the angular momentum of the star, L, decreases, on average, causing a decrease in <R>. As the orbit is tilted in the opposite direction, <L> and <R> increase. We derived a general formula for the oscillation frequency, omega, of orbits captured by libration near the OLR of the bar. It turned out that the ratio of the frequency of orbit oscillations to the epicyclic frequency, omega/kappa, is determined by the parameters q_R and <p>, where the factor q_R is the ratio of the amplitude of the radial force generated by the bar to the average radial force produced by the entire galaxy at the distance of the OLR, and the parameter <p> characterizes the average elongation of orbits captured by libration near the OLR.

## 159. [From Sky to Science: Time Domain Astronomy with the ILMT Survey](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14827) <a id="link159"></a>

**Abstract:** The PyLMT transient detection pipeline has been operational since November 2023 and has detected $\sim$10,000 transients and variable objects in ILMT images in near real time. The survey has enabled the discovery and characterisation of a range of bright and faint optical transients. The detected population spans a broad diversity of astrophysical phenomena, including supernovae, variable stars, active galactic nuclei (AGN) variability, and Solar System objects such as asteroids and comets. We provide a concise overview of the detection of a variety of transient and variable classes identified with the ILMT, emphasising the survey's potential contributions to a broad class of astrophysical sources. We highlight representative science cases, including supernovae, AGNs, variable stars, and asteroids, to demonstrate the scientific potential of the ILMT survey.

## 160. [Combination of multi-observatory photometry to determine asteroid rotation periods](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13223) <a id="link160"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.13223

**Authors:** Kristers Nagainis, Ilgmars Eglitis

**Abstract:** This study aims to determine asteroid rotation periods from sparse, unevenly sampled, multi-observatory photometry using the Lomb-Scargle (L-S) method. The photometric measurements of the asteroids are taken from the circulars of the Minor Planet Center (MPC). Twelve asteroids, four of them with known rotation periods, were analyzed with two complementary methods. In the separate dataset analysis (S-method), each observatory dataset was analyzed separately, and the final period was obtained as a weighted average of the consistent candidate periods, with weights based on the peak power of the power spectrum, the number of measurements, and the quality of the corresponding light curve fit. In the combined dataset analysis (C-method), all measurements were corrected for the biases of the photometric band, the star catalog and the observatory, reduced to a common scale and merged into a single dataset, after which the period was derived directly from the L-S analysis of the combined time series. Both methods produced consistent results, and they reproduced the known periods of the four test asteroids within 0.05%. The results show that the L-S method is well suited for finding rotation periods in sparse observations with uneven sampling over long time intervals. New rotation periods were found for asteroids 2968, 2971, 3081, 3473, 3716, and 4303. The period of 2968 remains ambiguous because of daily aliasing. For 3716, the most likely period is 10.474 hours, but its daily alias at 13.405 hours cannot be excluded completely. For 3173, the strongest peaks at 22.99 and 45.98 hours are probably daily aliases of a slower brightness variation. For asteroid 2607, the period agrees with a recently published value and is more precise. The approach is therefore promising for automated searches for unknown asteroid rotation periods.

## 161. [Unveiling photometric transients with the ILMT and their follow-up using DOT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18909) <a id="link161"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18909

**Authors:** Priyanshi Kumari, Bhavya Ailawadhi, Talat Akhunov, Monalisa Dubey, Naveen Dukiya, Sara Filali, Paul Hickson, Gokul Singh Mehra, Kuntal Misra, Vibhore Negi, Kumar Pranshu, Jean Surdej, Sarvesh Kumar Yadav

**Abstract:** The two 4-m class telescopes at the Devasthal site in India are complementary: the 4-m International Liquid Mirror Telescope (ILMT) is dedicated to the discovery of new transient phenomena, which can be followed up using the 3.6-m Devasthal Optical Telescope (DOT) for their optical characterisation. The ILMT continuously surveys a $22.3^\prime \times 22.3^\prime$ wide strip of the zenith sky. A dedicated transient detection and classification pipeline, PyLMT, based on optimised image subtraction and convolutional neural network (CNN) techniques, enables the discovery of numerous photometric transients with the ILMT. However, these transient discoveries still require spectroscopic confirmation to determine their types, for which spectra obtained with the DOT are essential. Here, we present a selection of photometric transients, specifically supernova candidates. We investigate their photometric and spectroscopic properties using ILMT light curves, supplemented with ZTF data and DOT spectroscopic observations.

## 162. [IceCube data for astrophysical neutrinos exhibit q-statistical behavior](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14129) <a id="link162"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.14129

**Authors:** Constantino Tsallis, Ugur Tirnakli

**Abstract:** Relevant IceCube recent data for astrophysical neutrinos are analyzed. Similarly to cosmic rays and proton-proton LHC collisions, the corresponding spectra are satisfactorily described, for the entire interval of measured energies, through q-Gaussians. This fact reinforces the already available evidence that nonadditive entropies (either of the $S_q$ class or of the $S_{delta}$ one, or even alternative ones) appear to play a crucial role in systems such as black holes and related cosmological phenomena (e.g., dark energy). The IceCube data yield sensibly high values for the momenta-distribution nonadditive index (q \simeq 1.6), in contrast with the cosmic rays values (q \simeq 1.2) and the LHC values (q \simeq 1.1). This fact neatly indicates very-long-ranged space-time correlations. The detailed associated physical mechanisms remain, at the present stage, as an instigating open issue.

## 163. [The School on Gravity: from motion to commotion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13514) <a id="link163"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.13514

**Authors:** Vitor Cardoso, Jose María Ezquiaga, Julio Parra-Martinez, Laura Sberna, Gustavo J. Turiaci, Lieke van Son

**Abstract:** Understanding the gravitational interaction is one of the great scientific endeavors of the 21st century. The Nobel Prize-winning discovery of gravitational waves in 2015, and subsequent detections by the LIGO-Virgo-KAGRA Collaboration, have marked a revolutionary step forward in physics and astrophysics by opening an entirely new window for exploring the Universe. Future upgrades to these detectors, along with upcoming facilities such as LISA, the Einstein Telescope, and radio and optical instruments, promise other breakthrough discoveries that the next generation of scientists will need to interpret. The School on Gravity (22--26 June 2026, Copenhagen) aimed to introduce junior scientists--graduate students and advanced undergraduates with a background in general relativity--to a wide range of foundational topics in the exciting new field of gravitational-wave physics. Topics spanned from the theory of motion, to new challenges introduced by quantum physics, to the astrophysics of black holes and source modeling for gravitational-wave detectors. The School on Gravity took place at the legendary Auditorium A of the Niels Bohr Institute, inviting students and lecturers to challenge current paradigms in gravitational physics in the spirit of the quantum revolution that unfolded in this very room. These lecture notes aim at opening up those new ideas presented in Auditorium A to the rest of the scientific community.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 3

## 164. [Particle Selection in Virtual Reality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17094) <a id="link164"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17094

**Authors:** Boaz Keren-Gil, James Gain, Lucia Marchetti, Patrick Marais, Alexander Sivitilli

**Abstract:** Point-cloud representations of 3D data are used in many fields, from astronomical surveys of galaxies to medical imaging and laser scans of buildings. A task common to all of these is interactive selection: picking out a meaningful subset of points for closer analysis. The data is inherently 3D, yet the traditional workflow uses a 2D mouse on a 2D projection and inherits the limitations of a flat screen. This paper compares Virtual Reality (VR) and desktop environments for 3D particle selection, asking whether VR's six-degree-of-freedom hand interaction yields measurable gains in accuracy, speed, usability, workload and flow. In a counterbalanced within-subjects experiment (N = 34), participants selected 90 target galaxies from a catalogue of 16,816 galaxies, using mouse-driven selection with keyboard camera control on the desktop and a hand-held spherical brush that adds or removes particles in VR. We measured task accuracy, completion time, and three standard questionnaires: the System Usability Scale (SUS), the NASA Task Load Index (NASA-TLX) and the Flow State Scale (FSS). Accuracy was comparable (91.6% VR vs. 93.1% desktop; p = 0.858), but every other measure favoured VR. Tasks were 23% faster (p = 0.001, d = 0.93), usability was higher (SUS 84.6 vs. 54.0; d = -1.78), workload was lower (NASA-TLX 20.4 vs. 34.7; d = 0.90), and flow was higher (FSS 83.2 vs. 70.2; d = -1.23). Because the underlying problem, making a 3D selection through a 2D interface, is not specific to astronomy, we expect the pattern to carry over to point-cloud selection in other fields.

**Novelty:** 3

## 165. [Harmonic and intermodulation emission from direct-to-device satellites - Harmonics of the Starlink 2.6 GHz downlink and the products around them](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14336) <a id="link165"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.14336

**Authors:** Balthasar Indermuehle, Benjamin Winkel, Federico Di Vruno, Gyula Jozsa, Liroy Lourenco

**Abstract:** Direct-to-device (DTD) satellites carry mobile base stations transmitting in terrestrial mobile bands. Their non-linear emission products can fall into radio astronomy bands, including passive bands. We measure the harmonics of the Starlink DTD downlink at 2620-2630 MHz and the intermodulation products around them, search for products next to the carriers, and compare them with the ITU-R unwanted-emission limits and radio astronomy protection criteria. We use autocorrelations of single Australia Telescope Compact Array antennas on tracked passes of DTD and other satellites with the 16 cm and 4 cm receivers. Harmonic ratios and receiver tests show that the harmonics are satellite-generated. We simulate the aggregate equivalent power flux density of a constellation at Effelsberg and the ATCA. The harmonics H2 to H4 at 5240, 7860 and 10480 MHz fall by about 16 dB per order, show no trend with elevation, and originate on the satellite. H2 is also detected in 32% of integrations more than 10 degrees from any DTD satellite. Inside the harmonic zones the spectra follow the n-fold convolution of the carriers; outside them, intermodulation products of order n+2 and higher lie on average 19 dB below the zone and switch between -23 and -15 dB within seconds. Next to the carriers no satellite emission is detected to 33 dB below the carriers. H2, >=-38.9 dBW in 4 kHz isotropic-equivalent, is at or above the SM.329 space-station limit; H3 and H4 meet it. For 8640 satellites the aggregate exceeds the protection criteria by 65 and 25 dB at the second and fifth harmonics, and by >100 dB for out-of-band emission at the mask level in 2690-2700 MHz. Harmonics are received far outside the serving beams. Their radiated level must be limited directly, not through the power at the antenna port. Compliance with per-satellite ITU-R limits does not protect radio astronomy from the aggregate of a constellation.

## 166. [Simulation Study of Reconstruction of Cosmic Ray Extensive Air Shower with Thermal Neutron Detected by ENDA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13183) <a id="link166"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.13183

**Authors:** Fan-Ping Li, Xin-Hua Ma, Hu Liu, Tian-Lu Chen, Shu-Wang Cui, Danzengluobu, Xin-Rui Gao, Wei Gao, Wen-Chao Gao, Hai-Bing Hu, Denis Kuleshov, Kirill Kurinov, Bing-Bing Li, Mao-Yuan Liu, Ye Liu, Igor Maliy, Da-Yu Peng, Yao-Hui Qi, Jia-Bo Qin, Dong Qu, Oleg Shchegolev, Yuri Stenkin, Yu-Sen Wang, Tian-Shuang Yang, Li-Qiao Yin, Hui-Qian Zhang, Liang-Wei Zhang, Shi-Yuan Zhang

**Abstract:** In the investigation of the "knee" region within the cosmic ray energy spectrum, extensive air shower (EAS) reconstruction by using different EAS secondary particles are of paramount importance for energy determination and composition separation and finally measurement of energy spectrum of different cosmic ray composition. The Electron-Neutron Detector Array (ENDA) is designed to detect thermal neutrons generated in the ground by high energy hadrons which constitute the "backbone" of EAS and carry vital information regarding the primary cosmic rays. At present, ENDA-64, consisting of 64 detectors, is employed inside the Large High Altitude Air Shower Observatory (LHAASO). This work simulates the response of ENDA and investigates the lateral distributions of thermal neutrons. A comparison of the NKG and double-exponential functions for describing this distribution using MC simulation data shows that the double-exponential function is more accurate. The mean difference between fitted and measured thermal neutron density is within ~ 6%. Meanwhile, the thermal neutron density at 10 m from the shower axis, $ρ_n$, depends mainly on the sum of all hadrons energy or the number of high-energy hadrons. The relationship between $ρ_n$ and EAS hadron energy or the number of high-energy hadrons can be well fitted by a linear function on a log scale. This work is fundamental for energy determination and composition separation with hybrid detection by using ENDA in the near future.

## 167. [Validation of Space Weather Forecasting at Leonid Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15213) <a id="link167"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.15213

**Authors:** Scott Shambaugh

**Abstract:** We validate Leonid Space's space weather forecasts through comprehensive cross-validation of retroforecasts scored against the entire F10.7 and Ap historical record. We built a unified forecast model that jointly forecasts F10.7 and Ap on all timescales from 3 hours up to multi-decade chains of future solar cycles. This was used to generate an archive of historical 15-year retroforecasts, after performing a leave-one-out fit of the model on held-out data and conditioning only on historical data available at the time. The accuracy of this archive was scored against measured space weather indices at all lookahead horizons from 1 day to 15 years, using the continuous ranked probability score (CRPS) as the primary quality metric. All other publicly available F10.7 and Ap forecast archives were scored using the same approach, including NASA's MSAFE and Schatten forecasts, SWPC's 27-day, 45-day, and Solar Cycle predictions, SWPC/USAF's RSGA, SIDC's Ursigram, CLS, Athens, KSO, BGS, SEPC's 3-day and 27-day forecasts, AFRL/NSO's SIFT, and BoM. Taken together, these 15 flux forecasts and 12 Ap forecasts compared on common lookahead metrics make up the most comprehensive cross-comparison of space weather forecasts we are aware of. Over a common forecast interval since 2000, Leonid had the lowest CRPS prediction error of any F10.7 or Ap prediction model: strictly lowest for Ap at all lookaheads and for F10.7 up to 5 years, and parity with MSAFE after 5 years. Much of this performance comes from our well-calibrated uncertainty bands, and we urge space weather forecasters to include calibrated uncertainties with their predictions and to issue retroforecast archives that can be used to rigorously evaluate their generating models.

## 168. [Insights from the stability of methanol maser flare properties I: G9.62+0.20E](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13774) <a id="link168"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.13774

**Authors:** S. P. van den Heever, D. J. van der Walt

**Abstract:** The brightest 6.7 GHz methanol maser in the Galaxy is associated with the high-mass star-forming region (HMSFR) G009.62+0.20E. Additionally, this methanol maser shows periodic flaring observed at 6.7 and 12.2 GHz. Several models have been hypothesized to explain the periodicity, including periodic accretion, spiral shocks in the gaps of circumbinary disks, pulsating stars, dicke's superradiance, and a colliding wind binary (CWB) system. We analyzed the time series of the 1.22 km s-1 feature of the 6.7 GHz methanol maser spectrum and the 1.25 km s-1 feature of the 12.2 GHz methanol maser spectrum over the monitoring duration of roughly 20 years, to determine an updated period, analyze the stability and repeatability of the flares, the temporal behavior of the flare profile, and the duty cycle of the flare profile based on the updated period. From these results, it is argued for or against any of the mechanisms that have been proposed to explain the periodicity. Lomb-Scargle (LS) and phase dispersion minimization (PDM) methods were used to determine the methanol maser period at 6.7 and 12.2 GHz. Once the period has been determined, phase-folding or period-folding was used to analyze the flare shape, flare stability, and duty cycle of the flares. The periods of the 6.7 and 12.2 GHz methanol masers were determined to be ~ 243.0 +- 0.3 days. The flares are highly repeatable, stable, and show almost identical shapes, as well as their duty cycles. A replicated kernel density estimator (KDE) of the flare shape reproduces the time series very well over a time span of ~ 8000 days. After careful consideration of the proposed mechanisms for periodic methanol masers, we conclude that all but one, the CWB model, have been ruled out as a possible driver of this flare profile. It has also been established that the CWB model can qualitatively explain the flare profile of the periodic methanol masers.

**Authors:** Nadia Dachlythra, Gabriele Coppi, Ludovico Bizzarri, Federico Nati, Alessandro Novelli, Elle C. Shaw, Samuel Day-Weiss, Federico Astori, Susanna Azzoni, Dillon J. Bass, Artem Basyrov, Federico Cacciotti, Rolando Dünner Planella, Nicholas Galitzki, Remington Gerras, John C. Groh, Jon E. Gudmundsson, Saianeesh K. Haridas, Kathleen Harrington, Carlos Hervías-Caimapo, Bradley R. Johnson, Brian Keating, Adrian T. Lee, Anto I. Lonappan, Noemi Mezzanzanica, Lyman Page, Giulia Rancati Cattaneo, Sara M. **Abstract:** We present a far-field beam calibration of the Simons Observatory (SO) Small Aperture Telescopes (SATs) using scanned single-frequency measurements of a drone-mounted source emitting in the 90 GHz band, where the SATs have an angular resolution of $\sim$30$'$. We then compare the results from two campaigns in April and December 2025 to the band-averaged beams from planet observations by the SATs. Measuring the telescope beam response at different frequencies across the observing bands enables modeling the system's frequency-dependent behavior. This helps track optical non-idealities. The comparison with planet beams informs the frequency resolution necessary to accurately reconstruct the band-averaged beams in this way; it uses only the average drone and planet products, as it is intended to demonstrate the feasibility of the frequency-resolved calibration scheme rather than to set the precise beam covariance and instrument requirements. We find that the beams of the two SATs near 90 GHz follow the frequency scaling expected for diffraction-limited optics, with only modest deviations observed in the nearest lobes of one telescope. We establish consistency between the real band-averaged planet beams and the synthesized drone band-averaged beams for the center wafers of both telescopes, with an average deviation smaller than 4% for multipoles $\ell < 500$.

## 170. [Institutional broadening of the Japanese astronomy and astrophysics research community during 1996-2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16633) <a id="link170"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16633

**Authors:** Hideaki Fujiwara

**Abstract:** Using bibliometric data from publication records, we investigate the institutional broadening of Japanese astronomy and astrophysics over 1996-2025. The number of Japan-affiliated publications increased from 588 to 2,156, and the number of unique participating Japanese institutions increased from 123 to 227, indicating a nominal broadening of the institutional base. However, institution-publication links remained concentrated among leading institutions, and the effective number of institutions, a frequency-weighted diversity measure, increased more gradually than the number of unique participating institutions. These results show that Japanese astronomy and astrophysics broadened institutionally, but not through a complete equalization of institutional participation. The observed change is best interpreted as an expansion of the participating institutional base around a persistent core of leading institutions.

# Paper Selection Prompt

## System Prompt

> You are a helpful paper reading assistant whose job is to read daily posts from ArXiv and rank the papers in order according to your friend's interests.
> Your job is to carefully read the paper titles and abstracts below and determine how well they match the criteria below.

## User Prompt

> ## Instructions
> 
> Write the response in JSONL format with {ARXIVID, COMMENT, RELEVANCE, NOVELTY} on each line, one for each paper.
> 
> - ARXIVID: should be the ArXiv ID.
> - COMMENT: for papers with RELEVANCE 7-10, briefly identify the specific matching criterion in at most 10 words. For papers with RELEVANCE 1-6, use an empty string. Never explain why a paper is irrelevant.
> - RELEVANCE: should be a score from 1-10.
> - NOVELTY: should be a score from 1-10.
> 
> 
> ## Scoring Criteria
> 
> > The "Relevance" score measures how closely the paper aligns with the core topics of the prompt.
> > The "Novelty" score assesses the originality and impact of the paper.
> > They are two **ORTHONORMAL** axes and **SHOULD NOT** be confused with each other.
> > When determining the final ranking of papers, the relevance score should be given twice as much weight as the novelty score.
> 
> ### Relevance Scoring
> 
> - Relevance 9-10 (Completely Relevant)
>   - Focus: Fully aligned with core topics with no deviation, score the highest if contains relevant keywords in it.
>   - Examples: Papers focused on foundational methods or theoretical research whose topic directly relates to core topics.
> 
> - Relevance 7-8 (Relevant)
>   - Focus: Retain a solid link to the main research area, though may touch on peripheral elements.
>   - Examples: Papers whose topics generally align with the core topics, but may other aspects or applications not directly related to the core topics.
> 
> - Relevance 5-6 (Borderline)
>   - Focus: Maintains a link to the core topics but also extends into at least one other domain/area beyond the primary focus.
>   - Examples: Papers whose results have implications for the core topics but are not directly related to the core topics.
> 
> - Relevance 3-4 (Irrelevant)
>   - Focus: Largely outside our interests with only very weak association to our topics.
>   - Examples: Papers whose results may have limited impact on the core topics and are not directly related to the core topics.
> 
> - Relevance 1-2 (Ignore)
>   - Focus: Purely unrelated to our topics. Completely a different domain.
> 
> ### Novelty Scoring
> 
> - Novelty 9-10 (Breakthrough)
>   - Definition: Groundbreaking methods/theory/application introducing new directions or solving major challenges.
>   - Examples: Entirely new paradigm for core topics.
> 
> - Novelty 7-8 (Improvements)
>   - Definition: Substantial insights/enhancements, though not a full paradigm shift.
>   - Examples: Modifications on existing methods yielding significantly better results. Applications to new datasets that directly address the core topics. Re-analysis of old datasets using new methods that directly address the core topics.
> 
> - Novelty 5-6 (Borderline)
>   - Definition: Incremental contributions with possible long-term benefits, not immediately transformative.
> 
> - Novelty 3-4 (Tangential)
>   - Definition: Minor or domain-specific improvements with limited broader impact.
> 
> - Novelty 1-2 (Low)
>   - Definition: Methods/application not applicable to core topics.
> 
> 
> ## Papers
> 
> [PAPER LIST HERE]
> 
> ## Relevant Topics
> 
> Use the following relevance criteria when considering which papers to keep. To be rated as highly relevant, a paper must fit at least one of the relevant topics below. Papers which fit multiple categories should be rated more highly.
> 
> 1. Near-Field Cosmology
>    - Relevant: Astrophysics work focusing on dwarf satellite galaxies of the Milky Way, extragalactic satellite luminosity functions, measurements of dark matter density profiles (core-cusp), abundances of dwarf galaxies, signs of historic interactions between the Milky Way and other galaxies (e.g., the accreted stellar halo). Both theoretical and observational work is relevant.
>    - Irrelevant: Papers that focus on Milky Way properties like the stellar disk, bar, and Galactic center. Papers that focus on higher mass galaxies, like massive spirals and elliptical galaxies.
> 
> 2. Resolved Star Formation Histories
>    - Relevant: Observational work deriving star formation histories from observed color-magnitude diagrams of galaxies. Theoretical work measuring star formation histories of simulated galaxies is also relevant, but should be weighted lower.
> 
> 3. Star Formation Quenching
>    - Relevant: Observational work discussing the quenching of star formation in galaxies, **particularly dwarf galaxies**. Work on higher mass galaxies may still be relevant, but should be weighted lower. Theoretical work on the quenching of star formation of dwarf galaxies is also relevant.
> 
> 4. Stellar Models
>    - Relevant: Theoretical work presenting new stellar models, either stellar evolutionary tracks (i.e., stellar interior models) or stellar atmosphere models. Work presenting new tables of bolometric corrections is also relevant.
> 
> 5. Numerical Galaxy Simulations
>    - Relevant: Theoretical work presenting new numerical simulations of galaxies is relevant, especially high-resolution simulations. Simulations that include new physics models (e.g., ionizing radiation, cosmic ray transport) should be given increased weight. Work analyzing previously published simulations should be weighted lower, unless the work also pertains to one of the other relevant topics. Semi-analytic work on galaxies that matches the other criteria is also relevant.
>    - Irrelevant: Numerical simulations that do not focus on galaxies and do not fulfill other criteria are not relevant. Examples include simulations of tidal disruption events, black hole accretion disks, and planet formation.
> 
> 6. Star Formation Driven Outflows
>    - Relevant: Observational and theoretical work examining the role the star formation processes play on driving galaxy-scale gas outflows. Measurements of mass-loading factors are particularly relevant. Work commenting on the phase structure of the outflows is also relevant. Work on galaxies more massive than the LMC should be weighted lower.
>    - Irrelevant: Papers that focus on outflows driven by sources other than stellar feedback, such as AGN-driven feedback. Papers are relevant if they include discussion of both star-formation-driven outflows and other types of outflows.
> 
> 7. Circumgalactic Medium
>    - Relevant: Observational and theoretical work examining the circumgalactic media (CGM) of dwarf galaxies is relevant. Increased weight should be given to work that connects the CGM to ISM outflows from the galaxy.
> 
> 
> 
> **Keywords:**
> 
> - Relevant: Near-field cosmology, satellite luminosity function, core-cusp, substructure, resolved star formation history, satellite quenching, mass-loading factor
> - Irrelevant: bar, Galactic center, tidal disruption event (TDE), supermassive black hole (SMBH)
